5.3.10
Source condition in the language of interpolation space

Below, we present the description of this condition in the language of interpolation spaces, which is taken from
[
ZLL23
, section 2]
. For any
s\geq 0
, let
L^{s}:f\in L_{2}(\mu)\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}^{s}\left<f,f_{j}\right>_{L_{2}}f_{j}
, where we recall that
(f_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}\in L_{2}(\mu)
form an ONB of
L_{2}(\mu)
. Let
[{\cal H}]^{s}
be a Hilbert space defined as

\displaystyle[{\cal H}]^{s}:=\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\sigma_{j}^{s/2}f_{j}:\,(a_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}\in\ell_{2}\right\},\quad\left<f,g\right>_{[{\cal H}]^{s}}:=\left<L^{-s/2}f,L^{-s/2}g\right>_{L^{2}(\mu)}.

We have:

\displaystyle\left\|f^{*}\right\|_{[{\cal H}]^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}\sigma_{j}^{1/2}\sigma_{j}^{-s/2}f_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}\leq 1.

(28)

6
Proofs

We first provide some definitions. Define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \square&=\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}N}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\\
&\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\sqrt{\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\},\end{aligned}

(29)

if
\sigma_{1}N\leq\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))
; and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \square&=\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\\
&{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\},\end{aligned}

(30)

if
\sigma_{1}N>\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))
. Let
\triangle=\square\sqrt{N}/\sqrt{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}
.

For any
\lambda\geq 0
and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define

\displaystyle r_{\lambda,k}^{*}:=\max\left\{\square,\sigma_{\xi}\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}}{\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\right\}.

(31)

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\max\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}{\square},\frac{1}{\triangle}\right)\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}.

(32)

For the sake of simplicity, we denote by
A:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\cal H}
, a random matrix with i.i.d. column vectors denoted by
\phi(X_{1}),\cdots,\phi(X_{N})
:
A=\left[\phi(X_{1})|\cdots|\phi(X_{N})\right]
such that for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
A{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\phi(X_{i})
. In other words,
A
is the adjoint of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}
, i.e.,
A={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}
. We denote
\ell^{*}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\phi(X)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}K(X,X)}=\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}
.

6.1
Proof of Theorem
3
(the
k\lesssim N
case)

In this section, we establish the proof of Theorem
3
. The proof is generally divided into two main parts: the Stochastic Argument and the Deterministic Argument. In the following subsection, we commence with the Stochastic Argument.

6.1.1
Stochastic event behind Theorem
3
.

Let
C_{\ref*{C_DMU}},{C_{10}},{C_{11}},{c_{9}}
, and
{C_{12}}
be absolute constants. We denote by
\Omega_{0}
the event which we have:

•

for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left(\frac{1}{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}&\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I\right){\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{3}{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\end{aligned}

(33)

•

for all
f_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq(1/\sqrt{N})\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f_{1:k}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}

(34)

•

for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\left(\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}

(35)

•

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\kappa\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

(36)

•

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:\infty}\right)(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right).

(37)

By the definition of
\tilde{\delta}
, see (
9
), there exists an absolute constant
{c_{10}}
such that if
\delta^{2},\bar{\delta}^{2}<c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}
, we have
\tilde{\delta}<1/2
. When
\lambda>C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
, one may replace the absolute constants
\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{3}{2}
in (
33
) by those in Theorem
2
. It follows from Theorem
2
, Proposition
1
and Proposition
2
that if
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H})
, then with probability larger than
1-\bar{p}_{RIP}-\bar{p}_{DM}-\bar{p}_{DMU},

(
33
), (
34
) and (
35
) hold.

For (
36
), we use
[
Men16
, Lemma 3.2]
on the
L_{r}
-norm of a sum of i.i.d. random variables to deduce the following result.

Lemma 3
.

There exist absolute constants
{c_{11}},{c_{12}},{c_{13}}
such that the following holds. Let
1\leq r<q
, set
Z\in L_{q}
and put
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{N}
to be independent copies of
Z
. Fix
1\leq p\leq N
, let
j_{0}=\lceil(c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}p)/\left(((q/r)-1)\log{(4+eN/p)}\right)\rceil
and
t>2
.
If
j_{0}=1
and
0<\beta<(q/r)-1
then with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}t^{-q}N^{-\beta}
,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left|Z_{i}\right|^{r}\right)^{1/r}\leq c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}\left(\frac{q}{q-(\beta+1)r}\right)^{1/r}t\left\|Z\right\|_{L_{q}}N^{1/r}.

Without loss of generality, we take
c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}>1
.
Let
Z_{i}=f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}(X_{i})
,
r=2
,
p=1
,
q=4+\epsilon
(where
\epsilon
is from Assumption
1
, Assumption
2
and Assumption
3
) and
\beta=1
in Lemma
3
, and by the fact that
\left\|f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{L_{4+\epsilon}}\leq\kappa\left\|f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{L_{2}}
, Lemma
3
indicates that if
N\geq(e^{c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}}-4)/e\vee c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}^{2}
, then there exists absolute constant
{c_{14}}
with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}/N
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}(X_{i})\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\sqrt{N}\left\|f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{L_{4+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\kappa\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}},

where
c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}=c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}=c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}\sqrt{(4+\epsilon)/\epsilon}.

We are left with checking (
37
). However, this is a simple consequence of Assumption
2
. By Assumption
2
, (
37
) holds with probability at least
1-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}
with constant
C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}=1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_L2}}.

Combining the above probabilistic estimates, we have the following Proposition

Proposition 14
.

Suppose Assumption
1
, Assumption
2
and Assumption
3
hold. There exist absolute constants
c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}
and
c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}
, such that if
N\geq(e^{c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}}-4)/e\vee c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}^{2}
, and if there exists
k\leq c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}N,
such that
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\bar{p}_{RIP}-\bar{p}_{DM}-\bar{p}_{DMU}-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}}{N}-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}.

We now place ourselves on the event
\Omega_{0}
up to the end of the proof of Theorem
3
. All the remaining material does not rely on any stochastic arguments since they all have been collectd in
\Omega_{0}
.

6.1.2
Decomposition of
\hat{f}_{\lambda}

As in the linear situation, the KRR estimator
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
is decomposed into two components:
\hat{f}_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}=\Span\left(\varphi_{j}:1\leq j\leq k\right)
and
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\in{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}=\Span\left(\varphi_{j}:\,j>k\right)
. The two components have their own role in estimating
f^{*}
:
\hat{f}_{1:k}
is used as a ridge estimator of
P_{1:k}f^{*}
whereas
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
is used to absorb noise, thus is not expected to be a good estimator of
P_{k+1:\infty}f^{*}
.

Proposition 15
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, the KRR defined by (
1
) can be written as
\hat{f}_{\lambda}=\hat{f}_{1:k}+\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
, where

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{1:k}\in\underset{f_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}}{\argmin}\left(\left\|Q\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f_{1:k}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\right),

(38)

and

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k}\right),

(39)

where
Q:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}
is a bounded linear operator such that
Q^{\top}Q=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}.
Such an operator exists because
Q^{\top}Q
is semi positive-definite.

Proof.
The empirical regularized loss functional is defined as
L:f\in{\cal H}\mapsto\left\|\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}f\right\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}
. As
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
is a minimizer of
L(f)
, we decompose
\hat{f}_{\lambda}=\hat{f}_{1:k}+\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
and take the derivative of
L
with respect to the canonical inner product on
{\cal H}
at
\hat{f}_{1:k}
and
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
and set them to
0
, as a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+\lambda I\right)\hat{f}_{1:k}+{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\mathbf{y}

(40)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k}+\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\mathbf{y},

(41)

where
I:{\cal H}\to{\cal H}
is identity operator. Solving (
41
) gives

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k}\right),

which coincides with (
39
) because of the Woodbury formula

{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}.

For (
38
), we plug (
39
) into (
40
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+\lambda I-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\right)\hat{f}_{1:k}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left(I-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{y}.

Let
F=I-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}
. The above equation is then equivalent to

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}F{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+\lambda I\right)\hat{f}_{1:k}={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}F\mathbf{y}.

Applying the Woodbury formula to
F
gives
F=\lambda\left(\lambda I+{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)^{-1}
. As
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}F{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+\lambda I
is invertible (because
F\succeq 0
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \hat{f}_{1:k}&=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left(\lambda I+{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+I\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left(\lambda I+{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{y}.\end{aligned}

(42)

To check that (
42
) is equivalent to (
38
), we take the gradient of the convex objective function
f_{1:k}\mapsto\left\|Q\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f_{1:k}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}
from (
38
) and set it to
0
. This gives
\hat{f}_{1:k}={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}Q^{\top}Q(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k})
. Recall that
Q^{\top}Q=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}
, and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}Q^{\top}Q{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}+I
is invertible. Hence
\hat{f}_{1:k}
from (
42
) is the unique solution to the optimization problem from (
38
) and so (
38
) holds.

6.1.3
Estimation properties of the “ridge estimator”
\hat{f}_{1:k}

For any
f_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
, we define its (empirical) excess risk as follows (note that KRR’s empirical excess risk includes a regularization term):

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|Q\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f_{1:k}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}-\left(\left\|Q\left(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f_{1:k}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\right)

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\bigl<{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-f_{1:k}^{*},f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\bigr>_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2},

where we have used the fact that
Q^{\top}Q=\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}
,
\left\|Q{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}=\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}
from Proposition
15
and
\left\|f_{1:k}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}-\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}=\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}-2\left\langle f_{1:k}^{*},\,f_{1:k}^{*}-f_{1:k}\right\rangle_{\cal H}
.

We denote the three terms of the decomposition (
43
) by
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
,
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
and
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}
respectively:

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}

\displaystyle=\left\|({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(44)

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}

\displaystyle=2\bigl<{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-f_{1:k}^{*},f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\bigr>_{\cal H},

(45)

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}

\displaystyle=\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}.

(46)

We notice that of these three terms, only the multiplier term
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
can take negative values, whereas the quadratic term
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
and the regularization term
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}
are always positive.

We will show that with high probability,
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square
and
\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
, where
\square,\triangle>0
will be defined later. In other words, we want to show that
\hat{f}_{1:k}\in f_{1:k}^{*}+B
where
B
is the unit ball of the norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
defined as

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}:=\max\left\{\frac{\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}}{\square},\frac{\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}}{\triangle}\right\}.

From the definition of
\hat{f}_{1:k}
in (
38
), we know that
{\cal L}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq 0
so it suffices to show that for all
f_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
we have
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
. We denote the border of
B
in
V_{1:k}
by
\partial B
. Let
f_{1:k}\in V_{1:k}
be such that
f_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
. There exists
f_{0}\in\partial B
and
\theta>1
such that
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}=\theta(f_{0}-f_{1:k}^{*})
. Using (
43
), it follows from the convexity that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}\geq\theta{\cal L}_{f_{0}}
. As a consequence, if we prove that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
for all
f_{1:k}\in f_{1:k}^{*}+\partial B
, this will imply that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
for all
f_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
. Hence, we only need to show the positivity of the excess regularized risk
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}
on the border
f_{1:k}^{*}+\partial B
.

For
f_{1:k}\in\partial B
, there are two cases:

1.

\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
, or

2.

\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}=\triangle
.

We will prove that either we have
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
(this occurs in case [1]), or
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
(this occurs in case [2]). Combined with (
43
), this will show that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
.
To achieve this goal, we need to obtain a lower bound for
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [1] and an upper bound for
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [1] and [2]. The lower bound for
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}
is straightforward because this term is not random and is positive.

Bound of the multiplier term

We show an upper bound on
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
when
f_{1:k}\in f_{1:k}^{*}+\partial B
.

Observe that

\displaystyle\left|{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}\right|\leq 2\underset{f\in B}{\sup}\left|\left\langle{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-f_{1:k}^{*},f\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|.

Also, for
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
, we have
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\sqrt{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
where
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}
is defined in (
32
). Therefore,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
’s dual norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{*}
is also equivalent to
\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}\cdot\right\|_{\cal H}
’s dual norm which is given by
\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\cdot\right\|_{\cal H}
: for all
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
,
(1/\sqrt{2}){\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{*}\leq\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{*}
. Hence, for all
f_{1:k}\in f_{1:k}^{*}+\partial B
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-f_{1:k}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2}\bigg(\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

(47)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\bigg)

We next handle the first two terms in (
47
) in the next two lemmas.

Lemma 4
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
14
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\\
&\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right),\end{aligned}

(48)

where

\displaystyle\sigma(\square,\triangle):=\begin{cases}\square,&\mbox{if }\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\geq\square\\
\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(49)

Proof.
On the event
\Omega_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}=\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}},

because of the isomorphic property of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.

Hence, on
\Omega_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right),

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
\Gamma_{1:k}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}
.

We define the sets

\displaystyle J_{1}:=\left\{j\in[k]:\,\sigma_{j}\geq\left(\frac{\square}{\triangle}\right)^{2}\right\},\quad J_{2}:=[k]\backslash J_{1}.

They will match the definition of
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
in (
3
) once
\triangle
and
\square
have been chosen.

We prove the following lemma:

Lemma 5
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
14
. We define

\displaystyle t(\square,\triangle):=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\left(\square,\triangle\right)}\left(\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\right).

(50)

Recall
r
and
{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}
from Theorem
3
.
There then exists an absolute constant
C_{\ref*{C_noise}}
depending only on
{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}
and there exists an absolute constant
{C_{13}}
such that with probability at least
1-(C_{\ref*{C_noise}}/\lfloor t(\square,\triangle)\rfloor)^{r/4}-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

(51)

Proof.
Let
D=\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}
. We calculate separately the upper bounds for
\sqrt{\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)}
and
\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}
. For the basis
(\varphi_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of eigenfunctions
\Gamma
, and since
\Tr
is independent with the choice of the basis, on
\Omega_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\left\langle DD^{\top}\varphi_{j},\varphi_{j}\right\rangle_{\cal H}=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\left\|D^{\top}\varphi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j\in[k]}\left\|D^{\top}\varphi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}{\square}\vee\frac{1}{\triangle}\right)^{-2}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\varphi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}{\square}\vee\frac{1}{\triangle}\right)^{-2}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\varphi_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}{\square}\vee\frac{1}{\triangle}\right)^{-2}\left(\lambda+\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{4}\right)^{-2}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}^{2}N\sigma_{j},

where the last inequality follows from (
33
). Hence

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)}\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

(52)

This implies that
D
is a Hilbert-Schmidt operator.
Using the inequality
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\sigma(\square,\triangle)
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}&=\left\|D^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\\
&\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\\
&\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.\end{aligned}

(53)

We finish the proof by Proposition
27
with the
k
from Proposition
27
set as

\displaystyle k=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma^{2}\left(\square,\triangle\right)}\biggr\rfloor

and with
C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}=\frac{3}{2}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}
.

Bound of the quadratic term and choice of
\square
and
\triangle

In the previous section, we obtained an upper bound on
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
. Our main approach, as outlined in the previous section, is to separately prove
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [1], and
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [2]. Now that we have the upper bound for
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
, it only remains to bound
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [1].

Before we begin, we need to make another classification. This time, the classification is based on the values of
\sigma(\square,\triangle)
. In the upcoming proof, we will
firstly
start by classifying based on
\sigma(\square,\triangle)
, and
then
proceed to prove the desired propositions separately in cases [1] and [2]. This parameter is crucial in the analysis as it determines whether the regularization is too strong, potentially completely submerging the signal. One can revisit the classification discussion regarding
\sigma_{1}N
and
4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
in Theorem
3
. Doing so will reveal that this corresponds to the classification based on the values of
\sigma(\square,\triangle)
. When
\sigma_{1}N
is too small, it signifies excessive regularization that drowns out the signal.

If
\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)=\square

Let us first study case [1]. Consider
f_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
such that
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
. In this case, we show that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
. Notice that on
\Omega_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}&=\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\geq\left(\lambda+\frac{3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{2}\right)^{-1}c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\\
&=\frac{2c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.\end{aligned}

(54)

To prove that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
then make clear that is suffices to prove that the following conditions hold for well-chosen
\square
and
\triangle
.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right).

This is equivalent to
\square>\frac{12\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\kappa\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}},

which holds if
\square>\frac{24\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}},

which holds if
\square>\left(\frac{24C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\triangle\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}\right)^{1/2}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

which is equivalent to
\square>\sqrt{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N}}.

In conclusion, there exists an absolute constant
{C_{14}}
(for example,
C_{\ref*{C_square_1}}=\frac{24\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}
) so that if we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \square&>C_{\ref*{C_square_1}}\kappa\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\left(\triangle\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}\right)^{1/2},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\\
&\sqrt{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\},\end{aligned}

(55)

then
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
.

In case [2]. We consider a function
f_{1:k}\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
such that
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}=\triangle
. In this case, we show that
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
. Since
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}=\triangle^{2}
, this amounts to showing that

\displaystyle\triangle^{2}

\displaystyle>2\sqrt{2}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{2}\left(\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\right).

By Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
,
\triangle
must satisfy the following conditions:

•

\triangle^{2}>\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right).

•

\triangle^{2}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}}.

•

\triangle^{2}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}},

which is equivalent to
\triangle^{2}>\frac{64C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}^{2}N\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\right).

•

\triangle^{2}>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

Hence, there exists an absolute constant
{C_{15}}
(for example,
C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}=64C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}^{2}
). We need to choose

\displaystyle\triangle^{2}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa\max\left\{\frac{\sigma_{\xi}\square\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)},\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}N\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}},\frac{N\square\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)},\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}.

(56)

Then
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
.

While we have shown that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [1] and
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
in case [2] if (
55
) and (
56
) hold, our task is not yet complete because (
56
) and (
55
) do not explicitly define for
\triangle
and
\square
. We next derive explicit definition for
\square
and
\triangle
through these two equations.

We fix
\triangle
so that

\displaystyle\frac{\square}{\triangle}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}}

(57)

and take
{C_{16}}=C_{\ref*{C_square_1}}^{2}\kappa^{2}\kappa_{DM}^{-1/2}\vee 2C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa
and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \square&>C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\left(\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\right)^{1/2},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\\
&\sqrt{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.\end{aligned}

(58)

One may observe that the second term inside the max is different from the corresponding term in (
55
). However, if
\square>C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}\sigma_{\xi}\left(\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\right)^{1/2}
, and
\square,\triangle
satisfy (
57
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\square}{\sqrt{\triangle\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\square}{\triangle}\frac{\square}{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}}}

\displaystyle>\left(\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}}\cdot\frac{C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}\sigma_{\xi}\left(\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\right)^{1/2}}{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{DM}^{1/4}\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}}\geq C_{\ref*{C_square_1}}\kappa.

Hence the new choice of
\square
in (
58
) satisfies (
55
).

We now need to check that for this choice of
\square
, (
56
) is also satisfied.

•

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle^{2}}{\frac{\sigma_{\xi}\square\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\square^{2}\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}\cdot\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{\sigma_{\xi}\square\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|N}}>\frac{C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}}{\kappa_{DM}}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa.

•

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle^{2}}{\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}N\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\square^{2}\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}\cdot\frac{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}N\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}

\displaystyle>C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}\cdot\frac{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}N\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa.

•

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle^{2}}{\frac{N\square\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\square^{2}\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}\cdot\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N\square\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}>\frac{C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}}{\kappa_{DM}}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa.

•

\displaystyle\frac{\triangle^{2}}{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}

\displaystyle=\square^{2}\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}>\frac{C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}}{2\kappa_{DM}}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_1}}\kappa.

We deduce that with the right choice of the absolute constants, such a choice of
\square,\triangle
satisfies (
55
) and (
56
).

We have established that by selecting appropriate values for
\square
and
\triangle
, we can conclude the following: if
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in\partial B
, then we necessarily have
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
, and thanks to a homogeneity argument, it follows that for all
f_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
, we have
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}\leq 0
hence
\hat{f}_{1:k}\in f_{1:k}^{*}+B
.

In the beginning of the analysis, we assumed that
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\square
, which is true if and only if
\sigma_{1}\geq\kappa_{DM}\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}
. Hence if this inequality is satisfied,
\square
is an upper bound on the estimation error
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}
and
\triangle
is an upper bound on
\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}
. Notice also that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}=U\tilde{D}_{1}^{-1/2}U^{\top}
where
\tilde{D}_{1}^{-1/2}=:\square D_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}
. Hence we can express
\square
as in Equation (
30
).

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.

If
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}

In this case, it follows by definition that
J_{1}=\emptyset
,
J_{2}=[k]
,

\displaystyle t\left(\square,\triangle\right)=\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{\sigma_{1}},\mbox{ and }\tilde{D}_{1}^{1/2}=\frac{1}{\triangle}\diag\left(1,\cdots,1,0,\cdots\right),

where there are
k
ones in the definition of
\tilde{D}_{1}^{1/2}
.
Since we have completed a similar proof in the previous paragraph, we will expedite the presentation in this paragraph.

Suppose that
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}\leq\triangle
. As we discussed in the previous subsections, on
\Omega_{0}
,
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}\geq\frac{N\square^{2}}{4\lambda+6\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}
. To show that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

Recall that Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
hold true for all possible values of
\square,\triangle
, so we can still use them in the current setting. Hence:

•

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\kappa N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right),

which is equivalent to
\square^{2}>\frac{12\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\kappa\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}},

which is true since
\left|J_{1}\right|=0
.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}},

which is true if
\square^{2}>\frac{24\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\sigma_{\xi}\triangle\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{N}}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\left(2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

which is true if
\square^{2}>\frac{\sqrt{2}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}
.

We conclude that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{17}}=\frac{24\sqrt{2}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}C_{\ref*{C_noise_term}}}{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}}\kappa
, such that we can take

\displaystyle\square^{2}>C_{\ref*{C_square_3}}\max\left\{\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\sigma_{\xi}\triangle\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{N}},\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}.

Since
\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}=\triangle
, it suffices to choose

\displaystyle\square^{2}>C_{\ref*{C_square_3}}\max\left\{\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\sigma_{\xi}\triangle\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{N}},\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}\triangle\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}.

In the case where
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square
and
\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}=\triangle
, a similar analysis gives us that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{18}}
depending on
\kappa
such that we can take

\displaystyle\triangle^{2}>C_{\ref*{C_triangle_3}}\max\left\{\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2},\frac{N\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}},\frac{\sigma_{1}N^{2}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}\right\}.

(59)

Again, we choose that
\triangle=\square\sqrt{N/\left(\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))\right)}
. There exists an absolute constant
{C_{19}}
such that we can express
\square
as in (
29
).

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_4}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}N}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\sqrt{\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.

In particular, we check that for this choice,
\triangle
satisfies (
59
).

Proposition 16
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
3
, there exist absolute constants
C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_noise}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_square_4}}
such that the following holds for all such
k
’s and all
\lambda\geq 0
. Recall the definition of
t(\square,\triangle)
from (
50
), with probability at least
1-(C_{\ref*{C_noise}}/\lfloor t(\square,\triangle)\rfloor)^{r/4}-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Omega_{0}^{c}\right)
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}\left(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square,\quad\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square\sqrt{\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))}},

where

1.

If
\sigma_{1}N\leq\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))
,

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}N}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\sqrt{\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.

2.

If
\sigma_{1}N>\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))
,

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_4}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.

Remark 5
.

Since we are using a concentration inequality rather than a deviation inequality, we cannot determine absolute constants, such as
C_{\ref*{C_square_2}}
(for a discussion on concentration vs. deviation, see, for instance,
[
KZ20
]
). However, if we were to replace the concentration inequality with a deviation inequality, our proof could also establish absolute constants. For example, in our proof of (
36
), we utilized the concentration provided by Lemma
3
. If we replace Lemma
3
with Markov’s inequality, then (
36
) holds with a probability of at least
1-t^{-1}
, and the coefficient
C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\kappa
on the right side of (
36
) will be replaced by some
t>0
. Consequently, this additional deviation parameter
t
can be used to absorb the absolute constants accumulated during the proof, transferring them into the deviation probability and ensuring that the coefficients in the rate are explicit.

6.1.4
Upper bound on
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}

We do not expect
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
to be a good estimator of
f^{*}_{k+1:\infty}
because the minimum
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\cal H}
-norm estimator
\hat{f}
is using the ’remaining part’ of
{\cal H}
endowed by the eigenfunctions
(\varphi_{j})_{j\geq k+1}
of
\Gamma
(we denoted this space by
{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}
) to absorb the influence of noise introduced by
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
and not to estimate
f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}
which is why we call the error term
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}
a price for noise absorption instead of an estimation error. A consequence is that we can only upper bound this term by

\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

We then just need to find a high probability upper bound on
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}
.

We have for
A:={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}=\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}

(60)

and now we obtain high probability upper bounds on the three terms in (
60
).

On
\Omega_{0}
, for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\left(\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}.

(61)

Notice that this result holds without any extra assumption on
N
. We have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}\\
&=\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}\\
&\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq 4C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\frac{\left(\sqrt{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}.\end{aligned}

(62)

On
\Omega_{0}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\\
&\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq 4C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\kappa\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.\end{aligned}

(63)

Finally, let
D=\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A
. As
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\Tr\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)=\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\right\|_{HS}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:\infty}\right)(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}

is the sum of
N
i.i.d. random variables appearing in (
37
). On
\Omega_{0}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:\infty}\right)(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)
.
So

\displaystyle\Tr(DD^{\top})=\Tr\left(D^{\top}D\right)\leq\frac{\Tr\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right)}{\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}}\leq\frac{16C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})\right)^{2}}

(64)

and

\displaystyle\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}\left(\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right).

(65)

Set
k
from Proposition
27
as

\displaystyle k=\left\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}}\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}}{\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}\right\rfloor^{2}=:C_{\ref*{C_noise}}(\bar{p}_{\xi})^{-4/r}.

(66)

Then by Proposition
27
, with probability at least
1-\bar{p}_{\xi}-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{\xi}\frac{\sqrt{16C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}.

(67)

Let us summarize the above discussion into the following Proposition:

Proposition 17
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
3
. The following then holds for all such
k
’s and all
\lambda\geq 0
. With probability at least
1-\bar{p}_{\xi}-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\frac{\left(\sqrt{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+4C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}\kappa\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{\xi}\frac{\sqrt{16C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi}}N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}}{4\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}.

(68)

6.2
Proof of Theorem
6
(the case
k
is not necessarily smaller than
N
)

We divide the proof into two cases: either
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\square
or
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\triangle\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}
. Within each case, we further examine two specific scenarios, as discussed in Section
6.1
, labeled as case [1] and case [2].

In each of these two cases, we will prove separately that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
and
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
, thereby showing that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
when
f_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
. This implies that
\hat{f}_{1:k}
cannot be outside of
f_{1:k}^{*}+B
. See Section
6.1
for a more detailed explanation. The main difference with the proof of Theorem
3
is that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
no longer acts as an isomorphism on
{\cal H}_{1:k}
but it may act as an RIP on a cone defined in (
15
).

6.2.1
Stochastic Argument

The stochastic argument of the proof of Theorem
6
is almost the same as that of Theorem
3
, except that we make use of an
restricted
isomorphy property on the
\mathrm{cone}\left({\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))\right)
(see (
15
)) instead of the isomorphy property on
{\cal H}_{1:k}
. As a consequence, we shall prove that a vector (to be defined later) belongs to this cone. This requires some extra work in addition to the proof of Theorem
3
.

In this subsection, we prove that there exist absolute constants
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}
,
{C_{20}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}
and
{C_{21}}
, such that the following random event
\Omega^{\prime}
happens with high probability:

•

for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I\right){\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{3}{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

(69)

•

for any
f_{1:k}\in\mathrm{cone}\left({\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))\right)
,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H},

(70)

•

for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}\left(\sqrt{\square^{2}\left|J_{1}\right|+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}+\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

(71)

where
\triangle=\square\sqrt{\frac{N}{\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))}}
,

•

for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\left(\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

(72)

•

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\kappa\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

(73)

•

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:\infty}\right)(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_infty}}N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right).

(74)

Proposition 18
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
6
, there exist absolute constants
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_infty}}
such that we have
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega^{\prime})\geq 1-\bar{p}_{DM}-2\bar{p}_{DMU}-\bar{p}_{RIP}-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}}{N}
.

Proof.

•

Notice that Assumption
1
is granted in Theorem

. As in the proof of (

), there exists an absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}
such that when
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
(which is exactly one of the assumptions of Theorem

), then
\tilde{\delta}
defined in (

) is strictly smaller than
1/2
. (

) thus follows by applying Theorem
2
.

•

(

) follows by Proposition
3
. In fact, we can take
\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}
from Proposition

to be
1/100
(thus

with probability at least

.

•

Proving (
71
) makes use of Assumption
8
. Replacing
\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}
by
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}
by
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
in (

) from Section

leads to the fact that (

) with probability at least
1-\bar{p}_{DMU}
with
\bar{p}_{DMU}=\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_DMU}}}{N}+{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_RIP_k>N}}}
, and
C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}=C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}
. Moreover, recalling the definition of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}
(see (
32
)) the spectrum of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}
is

\displaystyle\frac{\square\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}}{\max\{\sqrt{\sigma_{1}},\square/\triangle\}},\cdots,\frac{\square\sqrt{\sigma_{k}}}{\max\{\sqrt{\sigma_{k}},\square/\triangle\}},0,\cdots.

Squaring everything and summing them together gives us the trace of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1}\Gamma_{1:k}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Tr\left(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1}\Gamma_{1:k}\right)=\sum_{j\in J_{1}}\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}=\square^{2}\left|J_{1}\right|+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}.

(75)

•

(
72
) and (
73
) follow from the same idea as in the proof of Proposition
14
as (

), (

) and (
73
) do not depend on
{\cal H}_{1:k}
. As a result, (

) and (

) hold with probability at least
1-\bar{p}_{DMU}-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_bX_f_star}}}{N}
.

•

By Assumption
2
, (
74
) holds with probability at least
1-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}
with constant
C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_infty}}=1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_L2}}.

Up to the end of the proof, we put ourselves on the event
\Omega^{\prime}
.

6.2.2
Deterministic Argument

As in the proof of Theorem
3
, the overall idea is to decompose
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
into two components:
\hat{f}_{1:k}+\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
, used respectively to estimate
f_{1:k}^{*}
and to absorb noise. Let us remind the ideas briefly.

•

The estimation property of
\hat{f}_{1:k}
: We employ the idea from Section
6.1
, that is, to decompose
\partial B
into two categories:
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square,\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
and
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square,\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}=\triangle
. In each case, we prove that either we have
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
or we have
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
as long as
\square,\triangle
are larger than its correct level. These indicate that
{\cal L}_{f_{1:k}}>0
(refer to (
43
)). By the definition of
\hat{f}_{1:k}
, this means that
\hat{f}_{1:k}\notin f_{1:k}^{*}+B
. Necessarily, we must have
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square,
and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
.

•

The price for noise absorption by
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
can be quantified. We leave this part for the corresponding paragraph.

Estimation property of
\hat{f}_{1:k}

For proving
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
or
{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
, we must bound
{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
from above and
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
from below. As discussed in Section
6.1
(refer to (
47
)), we need to establish the analog of Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
in the case where
k\gtrsim N
, that is, to find high-probability upper bounds for the following two quantities:

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H},\mbox{ and }

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

The difference between the case where
k\lesssim N
is that we no longer have the isomorphy property ((
34
)), thus we have to deal with
\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}
by using another method. Fortunately, (
71
) exactly serves as the tool that we want. One issue remains: there are two terms in the upper bound of (
71
), namely,
\sqrt{\square^{2}\left|J_{1}\right|+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}
and
\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)
. In fact, we have already discussed in
[
LS22
, Remark 2]
that the only interesting case is when the latter term dominates, that is,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\square^{2}\left|J_{1}\right|+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}\leq\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle).

(76)

Further, recall the definition of
\square/\triangle
from Section
6.1
, that is,

\displaystyle\frac{\square}{\triangle}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}}.

(
76
) is equivalent to (
22
) in the assumption of Theorem
6
.

•

Lower bound of
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}
(Analog of (
54
).)

As explained in Section
2.3
, the kernel design matrix
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
does not behave like an isomorphy on the entire RKHS
{\cal H}_{1:k}
where
k\gtrsim N
, but only on a restricted cone
{\cal C}
(for the sake of simplicity, we denote
{\cal C}
for
\mathrm{cone}({\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))
) as defined in (
15
). We therefore have no choice but to assume that
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in{\cal C}
. To ensure
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in{\cal C}
, a sufficient condition is:
\square/\triangle\geq R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
. This is because under this assumption, and when
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square
,
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
, we have

\displaystyle R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\triangle=R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\frac{\triangle}{\square}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}.

This indicates that
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in{\cal C}
. Due to this fact, on
\Omega^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}&=\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\geq\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\geq\frac{4c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)},\end{aligned}

which is the same as (
54
) in Section
6.1
.

•

Analog of (
48
).
On
\Omega^{\prime}
together with (
76
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

which is also equivalent to Lemma
4
up to universal constants.

•

Analog of (
51
). Let

D=\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1},

thus
DD^{\top}:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
. Let us recall the following assumption in Theorem
6
: with probability at least
1-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_RIP_k>N}}}
,
\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq(1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_RIP_k>N}})\Tr\left(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}\right)
. This indicates that with probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}((\Omega^{\prime\prime})^{c})-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_RIP_k>N}}}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq(1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_RIP_k>N}})N\Tr\left(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}\right)
. Combining this observation with (
75
), we have:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Tr\left(D^{\top}D\right)}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\sqrt{1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_RIP_k>N}}}\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|D^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\cdot\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.

The upper bounds on
\sqrt{\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)}
and
\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}
are equivalent to those in Lemma
5
up to universal constants. Therefore, by Proposition
27
with the
k
from Proposition
27
set as

\displaystyle k=\biggl\lfloor\frac{\left(1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_RIP_k>N}}\right)^{2}\left(\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\right)}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_k>M}}^{2}\sigma^{2}(\square,\triangle)}\biggr\rfloor,

with probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}\left((\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\right)-\left(C_{\ref*{C_noise}}/\lfloor t(\square,\triangle)\rfloor\right)^{r/4}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{12\sqrt{1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_RIP_k>N}}}\sqrt{N}\sigma_{\xi}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

The above analysis indicates that the analysis of the estimation property of
\hat{f}_{1:k}
in the case
k\leq c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}N
also holds in the case where
k\gtrsim N
. We therefore have the following proposition:

Proposition 19
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
6
hold. There exist absolute constants
{C_{22}}
,
{C_{23}}
such that the following holds. Assume that there exists
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}
such that
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
, (
22
) holds, and
\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)\geq N\left(R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\right)^{2}
, The following then holds for all such
k
’s. With probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}\left((\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\right)-\left(C_{\ref*{C_noise}}/\lfloor t(\square,\triangle)\rfloor\right)^{r/4}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square\mbox{ and }\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}},

where

•

if
\sigma_{1}N\leq\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)
,

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_k>N}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}N}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\sqrt{\frac{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}

•

if
\sigma_{1}N<\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)
,

\displaystyle\square

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_square_k>N_2}}\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.

Price for noise absorption

As in Section
6.1
, we do not expect
\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}
to be a good estimator of
f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}
. We therefore use the simple decomposition:

\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}(\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

We then just need to find a high probability upper bound on
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}
.

We have for
A:={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\cal H}=\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A(\mathbf{y}-{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}

and now we obtain high probability upper bounds on the three terms. The second and the third term are dealt with in exactly the same way as before, as they do not depend on
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
. Our approach with the first term has to be different, however. From the decomposition of the excess risk and from the fact that
{\cal L}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq 0
, it follows that
{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}+{\cal R}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq\left|{\cal M}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\right|
. We have also seen that

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\geq\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(77)

We would now like to obtain a bound for
\left|{\cal M}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\right|
, so for all
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
, we define

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}:=\max\left\{\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H},\,\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}}\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}.

(78)

By convention, we set
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}=0
for
f\in{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}
. By Proposition
19
, it follows that on
\Omega^{\prime}
, we have
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\square
. Therefore if we define

\displaystyle\theta:=\sup\left(\left|{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}\right|:\,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq 1\right),

(79)

then it follows from the inequality
{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}+{\cal R}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq\left|{\cal M}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\right|
that

\displaystyle\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\theta\square.

Therefore,
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))\theta\square}
.
Since

\displaystyle\frac{\square}{\triangle}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}{N}},

it follows that
\square{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
. Hence if
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}
is the dual norm of
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
and
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}
the dual norm of
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
, then
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}=\square{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}
. This implies that
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}
is equivalent to
\square^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\cdot\right\|_{\cal H}
. From the definition of
\theta
it follows that
