\displaystyle\frac{\theta}{2}=\sup\bigg(\left|\left\langle{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right)-f_{1:k}^{*},f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|

\displaystyle:\,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq 1\bigg)

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right)-f_{1:k}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f_{1:k}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}

\displaystyle+{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim\square^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\square^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle+\square^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H},

which are exactly the quantities studied in the last paragraph, and we have shown that they are bounded from above by the right-hand-side of (
77
), and
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}N\square^{2}
due to the fact that
\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in{\cal C}
. As a result, with probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}\left((\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\right)-\exp\left(-t(\square,\triangle)/2\right)
,
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{N}\square.
The above analysis leads to the following proposition:

Proposition 20
.

Suppose that Assumption
1
, Assumption
2
and Assumption
8
hold. There exist absolute constants
{C_{24}}
,
{C_{25}}
and
{C_{26}}
such that the following holds. Assume that there exists
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}
such that
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
, (
22
) holds, and
\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)\geq N\left(R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\right)^{2}
, then the following holds for all such
k
’s and for all
t>0
. With probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}\left((\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\right)-\exp\left(-t(\square,\triangle)/2\right)-\bar{p}_{\xi}
(see (
66
)),

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\left(\hat{f}_{k+1:\infty}-f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref*{C_price_overfitting_1}}\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left(\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\square\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}+\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{\xi}\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.

6.3
Proof of Theorem
2

Proof.
We prove Theorem
2
, and for the sake of generality, we replace the covariance matrix by
\Gamma
, that is,
\Gamma={\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi(X)\otimes\phi(X)\right]
. For some
2<p\leq 2+\epsilon
, let

B=\sup\left({\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\langle\phi(X),f\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|^{p}:\,\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}=1\right).

(80)

Since we have
L_{2+\epsilon}-L_{2}
norm equivalence of marginals (recall (
7
)),
B\leq\kappa^{p}\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\text{op}}^{p/2}
.
To obtain a high-probability upper bound for Equation (
10
), we first use a one-scale net argument. Set
V_{\epsilon_{0}}=\left\{\pi\lambda:\,\lambda\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right\}
to be an
\epsilon_{0}
-net of
S_{2}^{N-1}
so that for all
\lambda\in S_{2}^{N-1}
,
\left\|\pi\lambda-\lambda\right\|_{2}\leq\epsilon_{0}
and
\left|V_{\epsilon_{0}}\right|\leq(5/\epsilon_{0})^{N}
. Unlike the situation in
[
LS22
]
, the choice of
\epsilon_{0}
here does not affect the probability deviation because we will use a different discretization procedure to control
{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}
(which will be defined later) uniformly on
S_{2}^{N-1}
, which does not depend on
\epsilon_{0}
. Therefore, one may choose an
\epsilon_{0}
as small as possible(say,
1/10
) to compensate for absolute constants at the end of the proof.

It follows from the triangle inequality that for every
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\|A{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\|A\left({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\left\|A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\frac{2\left\langle A\left({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right),A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\rangle_{\cal H}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|A\left({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\left|\frac{\left\|A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|+\left|\frac{2\left\langle A\left({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right),A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\rangle_{\cal H}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\right|

and so, from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\underset{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left|\frac{\left\|A{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|\leq\Phi^{2}+\Psi^{2}+2\Phi\sqrt{\Psi^{2}+1}

where

\Phi^{2}:=\underset{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\frac{\left\|A\left({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right)\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\mbox{ and }\Psi^{2}:=\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}\left|\frac{\left\|A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|.

Thus, we only need to bound
\Phi
and
\Psi
from above. To that end, we start with a decoupling argument to deal with the cross terms.

A decoupling argument

Let
(\eta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}
be
N
i.i.d. selectors (i.e.
{\mathbb{P}}(\eta_{i}=0)={\mathbb{P}}(\eta_{i}=1)=1/2
) and define
I_{\eta}:=\left\{1\leq i\leq N:\,\eta_{i}=1\right\}
. For all
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
and
I\subset[N]
, we also define

V_{I,{\boldsymbol{u}}}:=\frac{1}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\left\langle\left(\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right),\,\left(\sum_{j\in I^{c}}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right)\right\rangle_{\cal H}.

For every
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in B_{2}^{N}
, a decoupling technique (see for instance Chapter 6 of
[
Ver18
]
) leads to the following result

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|A{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}u_{i}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{i\neq j}u_{i}u_{j}\frac{\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}

(81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}u_{i}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}4\eta_{i}{(1-\eta_{j})}u_{i}u_{j}\frac{\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}u_{i}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+\frac{4}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}{\left\langle\left(\sum_{i\in I_{\eta}}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right),\,\left(\sum_{j\in I_{\eta}^{c}}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right)\right\rangle_{\cal H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}u_{i}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+4{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}},

where
{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}
is the expectation with respect to
(\eta_{i})_{i\in[N]}
conditionally on all other random variables. With the decoupling technique, we are going to estimate
\Phi^{2}
and
\Psi^{2}
from above. We start with
\Psi^{2}
and get

\displaystyle\Psi^{2}=

\displaystyle\underset{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left|\frac{\left\|A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|\leq\underset{\lambda\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\left(\pi\lambda\right)_{i}^{2}-1\right|+4\underset{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}-1\right|+4\underset{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\right|.

(82)

We next have
\Phi^{2}=\sup\left(\left\|A({\boldsymbol{\lambda}}-\pi{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}/\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\leq(\epsilon_{0}/\ell^{*})^{2}\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}
, where
\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}
is the operator norm of
A:({\mathbb{R}}^{N},\ell_{2})\to({\cal H},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\cal H})
, thus it remains to prove a high probability upper bound on
\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}
. From (
81
) we know that

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\leq\underset{i\in[N]}{\max}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+4\underset{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right\|_{2}=1}{\sup}{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}.

(83)

As a result,

\Phi^{2}\leq\frac{4\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}\underset{\pi{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in V_{1/2}}{\mathrm{sup}}\left\|A(\pi{\boldsymbol{\mu}})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq 4\epsilon_{0}^{2}\left(\underset{1\leq i\leq N}{\mathrm{max}}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}}+4\underset{\pi{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\in V_{1/2}}{\mathrm{sup}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},\pi{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\right).

(84)

Therefore, we only need to find a high probability upper bound on
\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}\right|
uniformly for all
{\boldsymbol{u}}
in
V_{1/2}
and
V_{\epsilon_{0}}
. In contrast to the method employed in
[
LS22
]
, we immediately derive this upper bound uniformly over
B_{2}^{N}
. This will result in
\epsilon_{0}
being a free parameter, as we will show at the end of the proof ((
92
) holds on
S_{2}^{N-1}
, instead of
V_{\epsilon_{0}}
).

To achieve this, we adapt
[
Tik18
]
’s argument. We recall that Theorem
2
contains two aspects:

1.

when
\Tr(\Gamma)
is the dominating term in
\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma)
,

2.

when
\lambda
is dominating.

In case [1], we need not only the upper bound of
\sup\left(\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)
, but also the lower bound for
\inf\left(\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)
. However, in case [2], we only need the upper bound for
\sup\left(\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)
. This will be clear in Section
6.3.2
.

We first introduce some notation.

For all
I,J\subset[N]
and
\ell\in[N]
, let
S_{2}^{J}
be the unit sphere in Euclidean norm of
{\mathbb{R}}^{J}
(see as a subspace of
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
endowed by the canonical vectors indexed by
J
), and let

\displaystyle S_{I}^{J}:=\left\{\lambda\in S_{2}^{J}:\,\lambda_{i}=0,\forall i\in I^{c}\right\}\mbox{ and }S_{I,\ell}^{J}:=\left\{\lambda\in S_{I}^{J}:\,\left|\left\{i\in I:\,\lambda_{i}\neq 0\right\}\right|\leq\ell\right\}.

For the sake of simplicity, we let
S_{I}^{N}:=S_{I}^{[N]}
and
S_{I,\ell}^{N}:=S_{I,\ell}^{[N]}
.
For each
k\leq N
and
I,{\cal C}\subset[N]
, we denote

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} g(k,{\cal C},I)&:=\sup\left(\left|\left\langle\sum_{i\in I\cap{\cal C}}v_{i}\phi(X_{i}),\,\sum_{j\in I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|,\,{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{v}}\in S_{[N],k}^{N}\right),\mbox{ and }\\
g(N,I)&:=g(k,[N],I).\end{aligned}

(85)

Further, for any vector
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
, and any
i\leq N
, we set

W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},i}:=\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H},

(86)

and for some
J\subset[N]
, we denote
(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},J}\right|)_{j}^{*}
as the
j
-th largest absolute value of the coordinates of
(W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j})_{j\in J}
. Given
1\leq k\leq N
and
I\subset[N]
, we define

{\cal M}_{I,k}=\max\left(\left\|\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{I,k}^{N}\right),\quad\mbox{ and }{\cal M}_{k}={\cal M}_{[N],k}.

In particular,
\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}={\cal M}_{N}
, and by the decoupling argument again, see (
83
)

{\cal M}_{N}^{2}\leq\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}:\,1\leq i\leq N\right)+4(\ell^{*})^{2}\sup\left({\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right).

(87)

Once (
87
) has been obtained, the next step is to derive an upper bound for the supremum of
\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}\right|
over all
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}
in relation to
{\cal M}_{N}
. The purpose of the remaining part of the proof will be to establish this point. In order to accomplish this, we begin with

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &4\left(\ell^{*}\right)^{2}\sup\left(\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}\right|:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\\
&=4\sup\left({\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}{\left|\left\langle\left(\sum_{i\in I_{\eta}}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right),\,\left(\sum_{j\in I_{\eta}^{c}}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right)\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\\
&=\frac{4}{2^{N}}\sup\left(\sum_{I\subset[N]}\left|\left\langle\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i}),\,\sum_{j\in I^{c}}u_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{4}{2^{N}}\sum_{I\subset[N]}g\left(N,I\right),\end{aligned}

(88)

where the last step is via Jensen’s inequality and by the fact that optimizing over
({\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{u}})\in S_{[N],k}^{N}\times S_{[N],k}^{N}
leads to a larger supreme.

A sparsifying argument

We begin by the sparsifying lemma for
g\left(N,{\cal C},I\right)
.

Lemma 6
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
k\geq 12/\varepsilon^{2}
,
4\leq m\leq k
, and let
I,{\cal C}\subset[N]
. There then exists an absolute constant
{C_{27}}>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} g(k,{\cal C},I)&\leq g(m,{\cal C},I)+2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)\\
&+C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\sqrt{k}\varepsilon^{-2}\bigg(\sup\left(\left|W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor m/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k}^{N}\right)\\
&+\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{z}},I\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor m/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k}^{N}\right)\bigg).\end{aligned}

(89)

As mentioned in
[
Tik18
]
, when compared to the findings of
[
BM22
]
, this method of sparsifying yields a lower cardinality when combined with the subsequent discretization argument. This gives rise to a broad condition about the tail of kernel features (from
L_{\log{N}}-L_{2}
to
L_{2+\epsilon}-L_{2}
).

The next lemma is a combination of
[
Tik18
, Lemma 13]
and
[
Tik18
, Lemma 4]
:

Lemma 7
.

Let
0<\rho\leq 1
,
r,h,p,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
r\geq 2
, let
V_{\rho}
be a support-preserving Euclidean
\rho
-net of
S_{[q],h}^{q}
. Further, let
T
be a
p\times q
matrix. Then

\sup\left(\left|T{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|_{r}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{[q],h}^{q}\right)\leq 2\sup\left(\left|T{\boldsymbol{v}}\right|_{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{v}}\in V_{\rho}\right)+\frac{4\rho}{\sqrt{r}}\sup\left(\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\left(\left|T{\boldsymbol{u}}\right|_{i}^{*}\right)^{2}}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{[q],h}^{q}\right).

Here, the support-preserving Euclidean
\rho
-net means that
V_{\rho}
is a
\rho
-net, such that for all
{\boldsymbol{x}}\in S_{[q],k}^{q}
, there exists
\mathbf{y}\in V_{\rho}
with
\mathrm{supp}(\mathbf{y})\subset\mathrm{supp}({\boldsymbol{x}})
and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{x}}-\mathbf{y}\right\|_{2}\leq\rho
. By
[
Tik18
, Lemma 4]
, there exists
{C_{28}}>0
and
V_{\rho}\subset S_{[q],k}^{q}
such that
\left|V_{\rho}\right|\leq(C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}q/\rho h)^{h}
, and
V_{\rho}
is a support-preserving Euclidean
\rho
-Net of
S_{[q],h}^{q}
.

A dimension reduction argument

Combining Lemma
6
and Lemma
7
with an induction argument, we obtain the following proposition, as a deterministic argument. Compared to
[
Tik18
, Proposition 14]
, we remove the condition
N\geq 128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}k
. This assumption
N\geq 128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}k
prevents us from being in the Dvoretzky-Milman regime, because when
N=k
, we need
\varepsilon
sufficiently large, however, Proposition
22
needs
\varepsilon
to be sufficiently small. Removing this assumption is possible because we are going to choose
k=N
in Proposition
22
, which keeps
128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k/(\varepsilon^{2}N)
as a constant in the proof.

Proposition 21
.

Let
I,{\cal C}\subset[N]
, and let
0<\varepsilon<1/3
and
k\geq 24/\varepsilon^{2}\vee N
.
Denote
t:=\lfloor\log_{2}\left(\varepsilon^{2}k/24\right)\rfloor
and define
k_{j}:=\lfloor k/2^{j}\rfloor
,
0\leq j\leq t
.
There are then subsets
V_{j}\subset S_{I,\varepsilon k_{j}}^{I}
and
V_{j}^{\prime}\subset S_{I^{c},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{I^{c}}
for all
0\leq j\leq t-1
such that for the absolute constants from Lemma
6
and Lemma
7
,

•

\left|V_{j}\right|,\left|V_{j}^{\prime}\right|\leq\left(C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}N/\varepsilon k_{j}\right)^{2\varepsilon k_{j}}
for all
0\leq j\leq t-1
.

•

We have

\displaystyle g(k,I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)\left(48+2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\log_{2}(k)\right)\varepsilon^{-2}\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2c_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\exp\left(\frac{128C_{1}k}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}\sqrt{k_{j}}\bigg(\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{v}},I}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{v}}\in V_{j}^{\prime}\right)\bigg).

Proof.
First, we let
j<t
and consider the quantity
g(k_{j},{\cal C},I)
.
By Lemma
6
, for
k=k_{j}
and
m=k_{j+1}
(where we have
k_{j}\geq 12/\varepsilon^{2}
and
k_{j+1}\geq 4
),

\displaystyle g(k_{j},{\cal C},I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(k_{j+1},{\cal C},I)+2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\sqrt{k_{j}}\varepsilon^{-2}\bigg(\sup\left(\left|W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{z}},I\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right)\bigg).

We first find upper bounds for

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right),\mbox{ and }

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{z}},I\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right).

We only obtain the former, as the latter follows from the same ideas.

From the definition of
\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}
we know that
\mathbf{y}
is supported on
I\cap{\cal C}
. By the definition of
W
(see (
86
)),
W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}=\left(\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\sum_{j\in I\cap{\cal C}}y_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right)_{i\in I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}
. Therefore, taking supreme over
S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}
is equivalent to taking it over
\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{I\cap{\cal C}}
.

Apply Lemma
7
with
r=\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor
,
\rho=k_{j}/N
,
h=\lfloor\varepsilon k_{j}\rfloor
, and matrix
T=\left(\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right)_{i,j}
for
(i,j)\in\left((I^{c}\cap{\cal C})\times(I\cap{\cal C})\right)
, then
T\mathbf{y}=\left(\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\sum_{j\in I\cap{\cal C}}y_{j}\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right)_{i\in I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}=W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}
and thus there exists
V_{j}\subset S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{I\cap{\cal C}}\subset S_{I,\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}
, which is a support-preserving
k_{j}/N
-net of cardinality at most

\left(\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}\left|I\cap{\cal C}\right|}{((k_{j}/N)\varepsilon k_{j})}\right)^{\varepsilon k_{j}}\leq\left(\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\varepsilon k_{j}}\left(\frac{N}{k_{j}}\right)^{2\varepsilon k_{j}}\leq\left(\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}N}{\varepsilon k_{j}}\right)^{2\varepsilon k_{j}}

since we can choose
C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}>1\vee\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right)\leq 2\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor/2\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4k_{j}/N}{\sqrt{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}}\sup\left(\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}\left(\left|W_{\mathbf{y},I^{c}\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{i}^{*}\right)^{2}}:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k_{j}}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor/2\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)+\frac{4k_{j}/N}{\sqrt{\lfloor k_{j+1}/4\rfloor}}g(k_{j+1},{\cal C},I),

where we have used
k_{j+1}/4\leq k_{j+1}
and the fact that for any
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
and
\ell\in[N]
,

\sup\left(\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}v_{i}:\,{\boldsymbol{v}}\in S_{I,\ell}^{I}\right)=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(({\boldsymbol{u}}_{I})_{i}^{*}\right)^{2}}.

The analysis for
\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{z}},I\cap{\cal C}}\right|_{\lfloor m/4\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},\varepsilon k}^{N}\right)
is similar. We obtain

\displaystyle g(k_{j},{\cal C},I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{32C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k_{j}}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)g(k_{j+1},{\cal C},I)+2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\sqrt{k_{j}}\varepsilon^{-2}\left(\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)+\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{v}},I}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{v}}\in V_{j}^{\prime}\right)\right).

Notice that for any
t^{\prime}<t
,

\prod_{j=0}^{t^{\prime}}\left(1+\frac{32C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k_{j}}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)\leq\prod_{j=0}^{t}\left(1+\frac{32C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k_{j}}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)\leq\exp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{t}\frac{32C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k_{j}}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right).

By induction over
0\leq j<t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{128C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}k}{\varepsilon^{2}N}\right)g(k,{\cal C},I)\leq g(k_{t},{\cal C},I)+2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}\log_{2}(k)\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\varepsilon^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}\sqrt{k_{j}}\left(\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)+\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{v}},I}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{v}}\in V_{j}^{\prime}\right)\right).

Finally, we bound
g(k_{t},{\cal C},I)
from above:

\displaystyle g(k_{t},{\cal C},I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\left|y_{i}z_{j}\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},k_{t}}^{N},{\boldsymbol{z}}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},k_{t}}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)\sup\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\left|y_{i}z_{j}\right|:\,\mathbf{y}\in S_{I\cap{\cal C},k_{t}}^{N},{\boldsymbol{z}}\in S_{I^{c}\cap{\cal C},k_{t}}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)\cdot k_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 48\varepsilon^{-2}\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right).

Stochastic arguments

We next introduce the randomness of
\phi(X)
. We will see in the next proposition that choosing
k=N
does not destroy the proof, but leads to a larger constant compared with
[
Tik18
, Proposition 15]
.

Proposition 22
.

For the absolute constant
C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}
from Lemma
6
and
C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}
from Lemma
7
, there are sufficiently large universal constants
{C_{29}}=(12C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}/\varepsilon^{3})^{36/\varepsilon}
for
\varepsilon<1/256
,
{C_{30}}
and
{C_{31}}
depending on
p
such that: Suppose
N\geq(12C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}/\varepsilon^{3})^{6}C_{\ref*{C_DM_3}}^{-\varepsilon/6}\vee\exp\left(48/C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\right)
, let
I\subset[N]
. With probability at least
1-C_{\ref*{C_DM_4}}/N^{3}
, for all
{\cal C}\subset[N]
,

g(N,{\cal C},I)\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}.

Proof.
Let
t=\lfloor\log_{2}\left(\varepsilon^{2}N/24\right)\rfloor
and
k_{j}=\lfloor N/2^{j}\rfloor
for each
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,t\}
. Fix
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,t-1\}
, we study the term
\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)
appearing by Proposition
21
for
k=N
. In the whole proof below
k=N
, in particular,
k_{j}=\lfloor N/2^{j}\rfloor
,
j=0,\cdots,t
and
t=\lfloor\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{2}N/24)}\rfloor
. At this time,
48<C_{\ref*{C_DM_1}}\log_{2}(N)
. For each
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}
, condition on
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
,
\left(\left\langle\phi(X_{j}),\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right)_{j\in I^{c}}=\left(W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right)_{j\in I^{c}}
are i.i.d. random variables. Recalling the definition of
B
, see (
80
), the conditional expectation of
\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right|^{p}
given
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
satisfies

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right|^{p}\big|(X_{i})_{i\in I}\right]\leq B\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{{\cal H}}^{p}\leq B{\cal M}_{N}^{p}.

Let
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}
(in particular,
\mathrm{supp}({\boldsymbol{u}})\subset I
). Condition on
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
,
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right|^{p}/\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\big|(X_{i})_{i\in I}\right]\leq B
. For
\tau_{j}^{p}=32eB\left(N/k_{j+1}\right)^{1+256\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}}{\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}}\geq\tau_{j}\bigg|\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\in I}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists J\subset I^{c},\,\left|J\right|\geq\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor,\,\mbox{such that }\forall j\in J,\,\frac{\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right|}{\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}}\geq\tau_{j}\bigg|\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\in I}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{\left|I^{c}\right|}{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}\left({\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},j}\right|}{\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}}\geq\tau_{j}\bigg|\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\in I}\right)\right)^{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{e\left|I^{c}\right|}{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}\right)^{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}\left(\frac{B}{\tau_{j}^{p}}\right)^{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\left|I^{c}\right|}{N}\left(\frac{k_{j+1}}{N}\right)^{256\varepsilon}\right)^{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}\leq\left(\frac{k_{j+1}}{N}\right)^{4\varepsilon k_{j}}.

Hence, conditionally on
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
, with probability at least
1-(k_{j+1}/N)^{4\varepsilon k_{j}}
,

\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}\leq\tau_{j}\left\|\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\tau_{j}{\cal M}_{N}.

Therefore, by Fubini’s theorem, with probability at least
1-(k_{j+1}/N)^{4\varepsilon k_{j}}
,
\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}\leq\tau_{j}{\cal M}_{N}
.
Taking the union bound over all
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}
(note that the cardinality of
V_{j}
is given in Proposition
21
) and using
k_{j+1}\leq k_{j}
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)\geq\tau_{j}\right)\leq\left(\frac{k_{j+1}^{2}}{N^{2}}\cdot\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}N}{\varepsilon k_{j}}\right)^{2\varepsilon k_{j}}\leq\left(\frac{k_{j+1}}{N}\cdot\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2\varepsilon k_{j}}.

Since
t\mapsto t\log{(eN/t)}
is increasing on
\{t:\,0<t\leq N\}
and
k_{t}\geq 12/\varepsilon^{2}
, we have for all
j=0,\cdots,t-1
,

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon k_{j}\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon N}{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}k_{j+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon k_{j}\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon N}{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}k_{j}}\right)\geq 2\varepsilon k_{t}\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon N}{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}k_{t}}\right)\geq 2\varepsilon\frac{12}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon N}{C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}\frac{12}{\varepsilon^{2}}}\right),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{24}{\varepsilon}\log\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{3}N}{12C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}}\right)\geq\log\left(\frac{N^{4}}{C_{\ref*{C_DM_3}}}\right),

when
\left(\varepsilon^{3}N/12C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}\right)^{24/\varepsilon}\geq N^{4}/C_{\ref*{C_DM_3}}
, i.e.,
N\geq(12C_{\ref*{C_DM_2}}/\varepsilon^{3})^{6}\cdot C_{\ref*{C_DM_3}}^{-\varepsilon/6}
.
The polynomial rate can therefore balance the union bound over
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,t-1\}
, which is a logarithmic rate with respect to
N
. With probability at least
1-C_{\ref*{C_DM_4}}/N^{3}
, for all
j\in\{0,1,\cdots,t-1\}
,

\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)<(32eB)^{1/p}{\cal M}_{N}\left(\frac{N}{k_{j+1}}\right)^{p^{-1}(1+256\varepsilon)}.

Finally, notice that

\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}\sqrt{k_{j}}\sup\left(\left|W_{{\boldsymbol{u}},I^{c}}\right|_{\lfloor k_{j+1}/16\rfloor}^{*}:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in V_{j}\right)\leq(32eB)^{1/p}{\cal M}_{N}\sqrt{N}\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}2^{j/p+256\varepsilon j/p-j/2}.

There exists an absolute constant
C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}
such that
\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}2^{\left(\frac{1+256\varepsilon}{p}-\frac{1}{2}\right)j}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}
for
1/2>(1+256\varepsilon)/p
. We finish the proof of Proposition
22
by applying Proposition
21
.

6.3.1
Case [1]: when
\Tr(\Gamma)
is dominating.

In this case, we apply all the aforementioned results (Proposition
22
, Proposition
21
and Lemma
6
) to
{\cal C}=[N]
.

Let us now apply Proposition
22
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{I\subset[N]:\,g(N,I)>C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{N}}{N^{3}},

so with probability at least
1-1/N^{2}
, there are at most
2^{N}/N
subsets
I\subset[N]
, such that

g(N,I)>C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}.

(90)

For these
2^{N}/N
“spiky” subsets, we simply use a deterministic argument: for all
I\subset[N]
, we have

\displaystyle g(N,I)

\displaystyle\leq N\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right).

As a consequence, if we denote by
{\cal I}
the set of all subsets
I\subset[N]
satisfying (
90
), with probability at least
1-1/N^{2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset[N]}g(N,I)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in{\cal I}}g(N,I)+\sum_{I\notin{\cal I}}g(N,I)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}2^{N}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)+2^{N}C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N},

(91)

We are left with an upper bound that has a high probability of
\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)
. We emphasize again that we do not use the sample coloring technique developed by
[
Tik18
]
, but instead, we use the strong concentration of
\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|
to absorb this
\log{N}
factor, because of (
8
).

Upper bound for
\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)

Let
p=2+\epsilon
.

For any
i\neq j\in[N]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|^{2+\epsilon}={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|^{p}\big|X_{i}\right]\right]\leq\kappa^{p}\left({\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|^{2}\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{p}\left({\mathbb{E}}K_{k+1:\infty}(X_{i},X_{j})\right)^{p/2}=\kappa^{p}\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2},

where we used (
7
) to obtain the inequality.
By union bound, for any
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle\right|_{\cal H}:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)>\tau\right)

\displaystyle\leq N^{2}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|^{p}}{\tau^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq N^{2}\frac{\kappa^{p}\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2}}{\tau^{p}}.

Let
\tau=\bar{\delta}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)/\log{N}
, with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-N^{2}\frac{\kappa^{p}\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2}}{\left(\frac{\bar{\delta}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}{\log{N}}\right)^{p}}=1-\left(\frac{\kappa}{\bar{\delta}}\right)^{p}(\log{N})^{p}N^{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma^{2}\right)}}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}\right)^{p}=:1-\bar{p},

we have

\displaystyle\max\left(\left|\left\langle\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right\rangle\right|_{\cal H}:\,i\neq j\in[N]\right)\leq\frac{\bar{\delta}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}{\log{N}},

where

\displaystyle\bar{p}=\left(\frac{\kappa}{\bar{\delta}}\right)^{2+\epsilon}\left(\log{N}\right)^{2+\epsilon}N^{1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{N\Tr\left(\Gamma^{2}\right)}}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}\right)^{2+\epsilon}.

Together with (
91
), with probability at least
1-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset[N]}g\left(N,I\right)\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}2^{N}\bar{\delta}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+2^{N}C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/(2+\epsilon)}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}.

By (
88
), we obtain that

\displaystyle 4(\ell^{*})^{2}\sup\left(\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}\right|:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\lesssim\bar{\delta}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\sqrt{N}B^{1/(2+\epsilon)}{\cal M}_{N}.

(92)

Combining Eq.(
87
), Eq.(
92
) and
B\leq\kappa^{2+\epsilon}\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\text{op}}^{\frac{2+\epsilon}{2}}
, there exists an absolute constant
{C_{32}}>1
such that with probability at least
1-\gamma-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
,

\displaystyle\sup\left(\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}}}\right|:\,{\boldsymbol{u}}\in S_{[N],N}^{N}\right)\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\bar{\delta}\vee C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\kappa^{2}\frac{N\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\text{op}}}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}\vee C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\kappa\sqrt{\frac{N\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\text{op}}}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}\cdot\frac{\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,i\in[N]\right)}{\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}

\displaystyle\vee C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\kappa\sqrt{\bar{\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{N\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\text{op}}}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\bar{\delta}\left(1+\kappa^{2}\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}+\kappa\sqrt{\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}\left(1+\delta+\sqrt{\bar{\delta}}\right)\right),

(93)

where we used that
N\leq\kappa_{DM}\bar{\delta}^{2}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
,
\bar{\delta}<1
and (
6
) from Assumption
1
.

Note that
S_{2}^{N-1}=S_{[N],N}^{N}
, and we plug (
93
) into (
82
) and (
84
) and take
\epsilon_{0}=\sqrt{\delta}
in (
84
),

\displaystyle\Psi^{2}\leq\delta+4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\bar{\delta}\left(1+\kappa^{2}\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}+\kappa\sqrt{\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}\left(1+\delta+\sqrt{\bar{\delta}}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\Phi^{2}\leq\delta\left(1+\delta+4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\bar{\delta}\left(1+\kappa^{2}\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}+\kappa\sqrt{\kappa_{DM}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}}\left(1+\delta+\sqrt{\bar{\delta}}\right)\right)\right)

Since we have the right to choose sufficiently small
\bar{\delta}
and
\kappa_{DM}
as long as (
8
) holds, we can set

\displaystyle\kappa_{DM}\leq\left(\frac{1}{12C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\kappa}\right)^{2}<\frac{1}{4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\kappa^{2}}.

(94)

Because
\delta,\bar{\delta}<1
,

\displaystyle\Psi^{2}<\delta+4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}\bar{\delta}+2\bar{\delta}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}\leq\delta+(4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}+2)\bar{\delta}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)},

\displaystyle\Phi^{2}<4\left(\delta+\delta+\delta\left(4C_{\ref*{C_DM_6}}+2\right)\bar{\delta}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)}\right).

Recall that in this subsection, we assume that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{33}}
such that
\lambda\leq C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)
. In this case, there exist absolute constants
{C_{34}}
,
{C_{35}}
,
{C_{36}}
,
{C_{37}}
and
{C_{38}}
such that

\displaystyle\Phi^{2}+\Psi^{2}+2\Phi\sqrt{\Psi^{2}+1}<C_{\ref*{C_distortion_1}}\delta^{2}+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_2}}\bar{\delta}^{2}+4\sqrt{\left(3\delta+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_3}}\bar{\delta}\right)\left(1+\delta+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_4}}\bar{\delta}\right)}=:\tilde{\delta}<1,

provided that
\delta<1/(100\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_distortion_1}}})
and
\bar{\delta}<1/C_{\ref*{C_distortion_5}}
(thus we can take
C_{\ref*{C_DM}}
in Assumption
1
as
C_{\ref*{C_distortion_5}}
), and where

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}=C_{\ref*{C_distortion_1}}\delta^{2}+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_2}}\bar{\delta}^{2}+4\sqrt{\left(3\delta+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_3}}\bar{\delta}\right)\left(1+\delta+C_{\ref*{C_distortion_4}}\bar{\delta}\right)}.

This proves that with probability at least
1-\gamma-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
, for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left(1-\tilde{\delta}\right)\ell^{*}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\left(1+\tilde{\delta}\right)\ell^{*}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

provided that
N\leq\kappa_{DM}\bar{\delta}^{2}d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
.

6.3.2
Case [2]: when
\lambda
is dominating.

When
\lambda>C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)
. In this case, we only make use of the fact that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}
is of rank-
N
and is positive semi-definite, hence
\sigma_{N}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)\geq\lambda+\sigma_{N}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}\right)\geq\lambda\geq c_{\ref*{c_distortion_9}}\lambda+(1-c_{\ref*{c_distortion_9}})C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)
, where we recall that
0<c_{\ref*{c_distortion_9}}<1
is some absolute constant. Hence our objective is to prove that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{39}}
such that with high probability, we have
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_1}}\lambda
. We prove this by proving that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{40}}
such that
C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_2}}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_1}}-1
, and with high probability we have
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_2}}\sqrt{\lambda}
.

Let
\{{\cal C}_{m}\}_{m\leq\chi}
for some
\chi\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
be a partition of
[N]
, by Jensen’s inequality, for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq\chi\sum_{m=1}^{\chi}\left\|\sum_{i\in{\cal C}_{m}}\lambda_{i}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}.

Applying (
83
) and (
88
) but with
A
replaced by its restriction onto
{\cal C}_{m}
for each
m\leq\chi
, we obtain that for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\leq\chi^{2}\underset{i\in[N]}{\max}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}+\frac{\chi}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{\chi}\frac{4}{2^{N}}\sum_{I\subset[N]}g(N,{\cal C}_{m},I).

(95)

At this time, we can make use of the sample coloring technique in
[
Tik18
]
. It is a technique used to truncate the inner products
\left(\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right)_{i\neq j\in{\cal C}}
. Given i.i.d. random vectors
\left(\phi(X_{i})\right)_{i\leq N}
and
H>0
, there exists an undirected graph
{\cal G}_{H}
whose vertex set is
[N]
, and its edge set is:

\left\{(i,j):\,1\leq i<j\leq N,\,\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{h})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,h\leq N\right)\right\}.

The coloring of
{\cal G}_{H}
is an assignment of “colors” to all vertices such that no adjacent vertices share the same color. The smallest possible number of colors sufficient to assign such a coloring is called the chromatic number of
{\cal G}_{H}
, denoted as
\chi({\cal G}_{H})
, and the collection
\{{\cal C}_{m}^{H}\}_{m\leq\chi({\cal G}_{H})}
is the associated partition by colors of
[N]
. That is to say, for any
m\leq\chi({\cal G}_{H})
, and
i\neq j\in{\cal C}_{m}^{H}
, the vertices
i,j
are not adjacent, so
\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|\leq H\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{h})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,h\leq N\right)
. Since
(\phi(X_{i}))_{i\leq N}
are random,
{\cal G}_{H}
is a random graph, and the following lemma is a high probability estimate of
\chi({\cal G}_{H})
. The following lemma is a weaker version of
[
Tik18
, Proposition 10]
, which is sufficient for our purpose.

Lemma 8
.

Assume that for some
p>2
we have
\sup\left({\mathbb{E}}\left|\left<\phi(X),f\right>_{\cal H}\right|^{p}:\,\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}=1\right)=B
. Then for any
H>0
and any integer
m>1
, the chromatic number of
{\cal G}_{H}
satisfies
\chi({\cal G}_{H})\leq m
with probability at least
1-(BNH^{-p})^{m-1}N
.

Proof.
Let us introduce an auxiliary random process
(Y_{i})_{i\in[N]}
with values in
{\mathbb{N}}
, where
Y_{1}:=1
as a constant, and for all
i=2,\cdots,N
,

Y_{i}:=\min\left(r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}:\,\forall j<i,\,j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+},\,\mbox{ with }Y_{j}=r,\mbox{ we have }\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|\leq H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H}\right).

The process
(Y_{i})_{i\in[N]}
is “classifying” each
\phi(X_{i})
is if
\left|\left<\phi(X_{1}),\phi(X_{2})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{1})\right\|_{\cal H}
,
\left|\left<\phi(X_{1}),\phi(X_{3})\right>_{\cal H}\right|\leq H\left\|\phi(X_{1})\right\|_{\cal H}
,
\left|\left<\phi(X_{2}),\phi(X_{3})\right>_{\cal H}\right|\leq H\left\|\phi(X_{2})\right\|_{\cal H}
, then
Y_{2}=2
(because
(1,2)
is adjacent in
{\cal G}_{H}
),
Y_{3}=1
, because either
(1,3)
or
(2,3)
is not adjacent in
{\cal G}_{H}
. Such a
{\cal G}_{H}
has chromatic number
2
.

By the definition of
Y_{i}
, we have that any two numbers
i\neq j\in[N]
such that
Y_{i}=Y_{j}
are not adjacent in
{\cal G}_{H}
, and
Y_{i}=Y_{j}
is a sufficient but not necessary condition for adjacency of
(i,j)
. In particular,
\chi({\cal G}_{H})\leq\max\left(Y_{i}:\,i\in[N]\right)
. Next for each
i>1
and
m\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{i}=m+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists j\leq i-1\mbox{ s.t. }\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H},\mbox{ and }Y_{j}=m\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H},\mbox{ and }Y_{j}=m\right).

For all
j=0,\cdots,i-1
, since
Y_{j}
is
\sigma\left(X_{1},\cdots,X_{j}\right)
-measurable, it is independent of
X_{i}
, hence

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{j}=m,\,\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H}\big|\left(X_{\ell}\right)_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{j}=m\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}}\big|\left(X_{\ell}\right)_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{j}=m\}}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\left\{\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H}\right\}}\big|\left(X_{\ell}\right)_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{j}=m\}}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|^{p}\big|\left(X_{\ell}\right)_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\right]}{H^{p}\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H}^{p}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{j}=m\}}\frac{B}{H^{p}},

where we used Markov’s inequality to obtain the first inequality. Hence

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H},\mbox{ and }Y_{j}=m\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{j}=m\right)\frac{B}{H^{p}}.

Further, by
{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m\right\}\right|=\sum_{j\leq N}{\mathbb{E}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{j}=m\}}=\sum_{j\leq N}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{j}=m\right)\geq\sum_{j\leq i-1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{j}=m\right)
,

\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|>H\left\|\phi(X_{j})\right\|_{\cal H},\mbox{ and }Y_{j}=m\right)\leq BH^{-p}{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m\right\}\right|

(96)

It follows from Equation (
96
) that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y_{i}=m+1\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m+1\right\}\right|\leq BNH^{-p}{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m\right\}\right|.

We next deal with
{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=2\right\}\right|
. We simply upper bound
{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=2\right\}\right|
by
N
. Therefore,

{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m+1\right\}\right|\leq(BNH^{-p})^{m-1}N.

Note that the set of values
\left\{Y_{j}:\,j\leq N\right\}
is an interval in
{\mathbb{N}}
, hence

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\chi({\cal G}_{H})\geq m+1\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m+1\right)\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{j\leq N:\,Y_{j}=m+1\right\}\right|\leq(BNH^{-p})^{m-1}N.

Combining Proposition
22
and the sample coloring technique from Lemma
8
, we obtain an upper bound for
{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}V_{I_{\eta},{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{v}}}
uniformly over all
{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{v}}\in S_{[N],N}^{N}=S_{2}^{N-1}
. We state this result in the following Proposition.

Proposition 23
.

There are absolute constants
{C_{41}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_4}}
depending only on
p
, such that the following holds. If
N\geq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_5}}
, then for any
\lambda>-\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)
, with probability at least
1-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
, where

\displaystyle\bar{p}:=N\left(\left(\frac{4\kappa^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{op}}{\Tr(\Gamma)+\lambda}\right)^{p/2}N\right)^{\lceil(8+2p)/(p-2)\rceil-1},

(97)

{\cal M}_{N}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{N}B^{1/p}.

(98)

Proof.
Let
H>0
which will be determined later, and let
0\leq m\leq\chi
, we apply Proposition
22
to
{\cal C}={\cal C}_{m}^{H}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|\left\{I\subset[N]:\,g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)>C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}_{m}^{H}\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{N}}{N^{3}},

then with probability at least
1-1/N^{2}
, there are at most
2^{N}/N
subsets
I\subset[N]
, such that

g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)>C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}\log_{2}(N)\max\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}_{m}^{H}\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}.

(99)

For these
2^{N}/N
“spiky” subsets, we simply use a deterministic argument: for all
I\subset[N]
, we have

g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)\leq N\max\left(\left|\left<\phi(X_{i}),\,\phi(X_{j})\right>_{\cal H}\right|:\,i\neq j\in{\cal C}_{m}^{H}\right)\leq NH\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,i\leq N\right),

where we use that
i,j\in{\cal C}_{m}^{H}
have the same color and therefore are not adjacent in
{\cal G}_{m}^{H}
.
As a consequence, if we denote by
{\cal I}
the set of all subsets
I\subset[N]
satisfying Equation (
99
), with probability at least
1-1/N^{2}
, for any
({\cal C}_{m}^{H})_{m\leq\chi}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset[N]}g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in{\cal I}}g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)+\sum_{I\notin{\cal I}}g(N,{\cal C}_{m}^{H},I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}2^{N}\log_{2}(N)H\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,i\leq N\right)+2^{N}C_{\ref*{C_DM_5}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N},

where we again used that
i
and
j
are not adjacent in
{\cal G}_{m}^{H}
.

Let
H=\bar{\delta}\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma)+\lambda}/\log{N}
with
\bar{\delta}=1/2
(unlike the case in Section
6.3.1
, we only need an
isomorphic
upper bound, we can choose
\bar{\delta}
to be an arbitrary absolute constant), then

BNH^{-p}\leq\left(\frac{4\kappa^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{op}}{(\Tr(\Gamma)+\lambda)}\right)^{p/2}N.

Let
\chi=\lceil(8+2p)/(p-2)\rceil
and apply Lemma
8
for
m=\chi
, with probability at least
1-\bar{p}
,
\chi({\cal G}_{H})\leq\chi
.

On the other hand, by (
95
) and the fact that (
99
) is valid uniformly over all
({\cal C}_{m}^{H})_{m\leq\chi}
(thanks to Proposition
22
), there exists an absolute constant
{C_{42}}
such that with probability
1-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
, for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}&\leq\left(\frac{8+2p}{p-2}\right)^{2}\underset{i\in[N]}{\max}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\\
&+\frac{\left(\frac{8+2p}{p-2}\right)^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\left(C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_4}}\log_{2}(N)H\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,i\leq N\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_4}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{8+2p}{p-2}\right)^{2}\underset{i\in[N]}{\max}\frac{\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\\
&+\frac{\left(\frac{8+2p}{p-2}\right)^{2}}{(\ell^{*})^{2}}\left(C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_4}}\bar{\delta}\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma)+\lambda}\max\left(\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}:\,i\leq N\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_4}}B^{1/p}\sqrt{N}{\cal M}_{N}\right).\end{aligned}

(100)

Solving (
100
) gives that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{43}}
depending only on
p
such that with probability at least
1-\bar{p}-N^{-2}
,

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{N}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{N}B^{1/p}.

Recall that we have assumed that
C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)<\lambda
in this case, and
\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda\geq(\kappa_{DM}/4)^{-2}N\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
. Moreover, since we have
B\leq\kappa^{p}\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{p/2}
for any
2<p\leq 2+\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{N}

\displaystyle=\sup\left(\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}:\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in S_{2}^{N-1}\right)\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}\lambda}+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\kappa\sqrt{N\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}\lambda}+\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}}{4}\kappa\kappa_{DM}\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma\right)+\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}}+\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}}{4}\kappa\kappa_{DM}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\lambda}.

Letting
C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_2}}=C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}}+C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_6}}\kappa\kappa_{DM}\sqrt{1+C_{\ref*{C_comparision_trace_lambda}}^{-1}}/4
, this is precisely our initial objective. As a result, we may let
C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_1}}=C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_2}}^{2}+1
, and
C_{\ref*{C_distortion_9}}=C_{\ref*{C_DM_lambda_dominate_1}}
.

6.4
Proof of Proposition
13

In this section, we are dealing with an inner product kernel, that is, a kernel that satisfies (
16
), and that possesses a smooth kernel function
h
, for which the associated RKHS may not satisfy the
L_{2+\epsilon}/L_{2}
norm equivalence assumption (
7
). Consequently, we introduce a new technique, involving further truncation of
\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}
. This technique stems from the following observation that RKHS with eigenvalues exhibiting power decay, that is,
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
for
\alpha>1
, possess the following property: for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\sim\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
. In other words, due to the specific condition of power decay, the contribution of eigenvalues
\sigma_{j}
for
j>2k
to the trace is negligible. Readers can refer to
[
BLLT20
, pp. 37]
for the proof of this property. In fact, for a general
{\cal K}>k
, we have
F({\cal K})-F(k+1)\leq\sum_{j>k}^{\cal K}j^{-\alpha}\leq F({\cal K})-F(k)
, where
F(x)=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}x^{1-\alpha}
. As a result,
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\sim\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\left(k^{1-\alpha}-(2k)^{1-\alpha}\right)\sim\frac{1}{\alpha-1}k^{1-\alpha}
. Moreover, by the same argument,
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\sim\frac{1}{\alpha-1}k^{1-\alpha}
.

The proof strategy is almost the same as that of Theorem
6
.

6.4.1
Stochastic Argument

Like in Section
6.2
, that is, like the proof of Theorem
6
(
k\gtrsim N
), we prove that there exist absolute constants
{c_{15}}
,
{C_{44}}
,
{C_{45}}
,
{C_{46}}
and
{C_{47}}
such that the following random event (denoted as
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
) happens with probability greater than
1/2
:

•

for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:2k})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I\right){\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}\log^{2}(N)\left(\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

(101)

•

for any
f_{1:k}\in\mathrm{cone}\left({\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))\right)
,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper_smooth}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H},

(102)

•

for all
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}\left(\log(N)\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

(103)

•

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},

(104)

•

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:\infty}\right)(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}N\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right).

(105)

Proposition 24
.

Suppose Assumption
4
holds. Then for every
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
(2k)^{\frac{1}{\log{d}}}\lesssim N^{2}/\log^{8}(N)
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega^{\prime\prime})\geq 995/1000-N^{-2}-2^{\frac{\log{(2k)}}{\log{d}}}\frac{\log^{8}(N)}{N}
.

Proof.

•

By Proposition
8
, the upper bound in (
101
) and (
103
) hold with probability at least
1-3/1000
, by setting
\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}=\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_2}}=\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_3}}=1000^{-1}
, and taking
C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}=10^{6}C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}
. Notice that due to the uniform distribution condition in Assumption
4
, Assumption
9
is verified with
\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}=\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_2}}=\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_3}}=1
, and
{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_1}}}={\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_2}}}={\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_3}}}=0
. See also Section
6.5.15
.

•

By Proposition
3
, (
102
) holds with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}^{-1}
. One may take
\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}=1000
,
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}=1/2
,
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper_smooth}}=3/2
provided that
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}<10^{6}/(8C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}^{2}C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}^{2})
.

•

By Markov’s inequality, (
104
) holds with probability at least
1-C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}^{-1}
. One may take
C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}=1000
for simplicity. For (
105
), we observe that they hold with probability
1
and
C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}=1
. This is, again, because
X
is uniform over
\sqrt{d}S_{2}^{d-1}
, thus
K
is a translation-invariant kernel.

•

For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, we let
{\cal K}=2k
. Notice that: the trace of
\Gamma_{k+1:{\cal K}}
is of the same order as that of
\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}
. We then separate the spectrum of
\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}
into two pieces:
(\sigma_{k+1},\sigma_{k+2},\cdots,\sigma_{\cal K},\sigma_{{\cal K}+1},\sigma_{{\cal K}+2},\cdots)
. From Section
6.5.12
, we know that
L_{2}(\sqrt{d}S_{2}^{d-1},\mu)
is spanned by spherical harmonics, which are polynomials defined on a sphere, that is,
L_{2}(\sqrt{d}S_{2}^{d-1},\mu)=\oplus_{l\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{d,l}
where
V_{d,l}
is the space of homogeneous harmonic polynomials of degree
l
restricted to
\sqrt{d}S_{2}^{d-1}
. By (
129
),
\dim(V_{d,l})\sim d^{l}
. As
\dim\left(\oplus_{l=1}^{\iota}V_{d,l}\right)\sim d^{\iota}
for any
\iota>1
, the eigenfunction of
\Gamma
associated with eigenvalue
\sigma_{\cal K}
for
{\cal K}=2k
belongs to
\oplus_{l=1}^{\iota}V_{d,l}
with
\iota\sim\lfloor\frac{\log{{\cal K}}}{\log{d}}\rfloor
, and thus
{\cal H}_{k+1:{\cal K}}\subset\oplus_{\lfloor\frac{\log{k}}{\log{d}}\rfloor\lesssim l\lesssim\lfloor\frac{\log{{\cal K}}}{\log{d}}\rfloor}^{\perp}V_{d,l}
contains polynomials of degree less than
\lfloor\frac{\log{{\cal K}}}{\log{d}}\rfloor
(up to universal constants).
Notice that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\succeq{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:{\cal K}}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:{\cal K}}^{\top}
. Due to the uniform distribution condition in Assumption
4
, (
6
) is verified with
\delta=\gamma=0
, thus Assumption
1
is verified with
\delta=\gamma=0
,
\epsilon=6
and
\kappa\lesssim 2^{\lfloor\frac{\log{(2k)}}{\log{d}}\rfloor}
.
Applying Theorem
2
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:{\cal K}}^{\top}
implies the lower bound in (
101
) with probability at least
1-N^{-2}-2^{\frac{\log{(2k)}}{\log{d}}}\frac{\log^{8}(N)}{N}
.

Up to the end of the proof, we place ourselves on the event
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
.

6.4.2
Deterministic Argument

The deterministic argument is almost the same as in Section
6.2
, except that the extra logarithmic factors appeared in the equations from
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
. Another distinction between the proof in this section and that in Section
6.2
is that, in the specific case of power decay, we only need to consider the situation where
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\square
. This fact can be derived through the following calculation: As
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
for every
j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
N\sigma_{1}\sim N\gtrsim\kappa_{DM}\left(\lambda+k^{1-\alpha}\right)\sim\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))
is always satisfied. Therefore, we only consider the case
\sigma(\square,\triangle)=\square
.

Estimation property of the ridge estimator
\hat{f}_{1:k}

Since many of the concepts and techniques employed in this section have already been utilized in Section
6.2
, we will omit excessively detailed procedures in this section.

As in Section
6.1
and Section
6.2
, by homogeneous argument, we only need to consider two cases:

1.

\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}=\square
, and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\triangle
, or

2.

\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\square
, and
\left\|f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}=\triangle
.

In case [1]

We prove that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal M}_{f_{1:k}}
. We first prove the analog of (
48
), (
51
) and (
54
). This time, we have to include extra logarithmic factors provided by equations in
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
.

•

For
f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*}\in{\cal C}
, on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
we have

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\right)}.

(106)

•

The analog of (
48
) is:
On
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

(107)

•

Analog of (
51
). Let

D=\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1},

thus we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Tr\left(DD^{\top}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Tr\left(D^{\top}D\right)}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|D^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\cdot\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\leq\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.

By Borel-TIS’s inequality with

\displaystyle t=\frac{C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}^{2}\left(\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\right)}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\sigma^{2}(\square,\triangle)},

with probability at least
1-{\mathbb{P}}\left((\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\right)-\exp\left(-t(\square,\triangle)/2\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{12C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

(108)

Notice that by Assumption
4
,
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
. As a result,
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\sim\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\sim k^{1-\alpha}
. Therefore, up to universal constants, we can bound
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)
from above and from below by
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
, that is, to prove that

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\log(N)\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{12C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\frac{12C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

•

\frac{c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower_smooth}}^{2}N\square^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}^{2}\log^{2}(N)\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)}>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

In case [2]

we prove that
{\cal Q}_{f_{1:k}}>{\cal R}_{f_{1:k}}
by showing that

•

\triangle^{2}>\log(N)\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\frac{8C_{\ref*{C_DMU_smooth}}C_{\ref*{C_bX_f_star_smooth}}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

•

\triangle^{2}>\frac{12C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}}.

•

\triangle^{2}>\frac{12C_{\ref*{C_sum_Gamma_phi_smooth}}\log(N)\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}.

•

\triangle^{2}>\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

As in Section
6.1
and Section
6.2
, we choose
\triangle
such that
\square/\triangle=\sqrt{\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)/N}
.
We check that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{48}}
such that we can take

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \square&=C_{\ref*{C_square_smooth}}\log^{3}(N)\max\bigg\{\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}},\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}},\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H},\\
&{\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{2\lambda+3\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}{N}}\bigg\}.\end{aligned}

(109)

Noise absorption

The price for overfitting is almost the same as that in Section
6.2
, except for two differences:

•

When dealing with
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(f_{1:k}^{*}-\hat{f}_{1:k})\right\|_{\cal H}
.
We no longer have (
77
), but

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\geq\frac{4}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}.

If we define
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
and
\theta
as in (
78
) and (
79
), by (
109
) and
{\cal Q}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}+{\cal R}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\leq\left|{\cal M}_{\hat{f}_{1:k}}\right|
we obtain
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}(\hat{f}_{1:k}-f_{1:k}^{*})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\right)\theta\square}
. However, since
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\sim\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
, we can replace the right hand side by
{c_{16}}\sqrt{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)\theta\square}
up to some small absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_useless_1}}
. The reminder part of the proof remains the same as in Section
6.2
.

•

When dealing with
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}
and
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}
. We once again use
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}\right)\sim\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)
. Moreover, we apply (
103
), (
104
) and (
105
) by including extra logarithmic factors. The proof readily follows.

Before concluding this section, we additionally state a property that allows us to obtain an upper bound without Assumption
4
and when
\lambda\gtrsim\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})
.

Proposition 25
.

Grant Assumption
9
. For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\lambda\geq 0
such that
\lambda\gtrsim\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})
,
N\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}d_{\lambda}^{*}(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H})
,
\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\leq\kappa_{DM}(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right))\left(1-\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|}{N}\right)
, and
\kappa_{DM}\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)\geq N\left(R_{N}^{*}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\right)^{2}
. Then with constant probability,
\left\|\hat{f}_{\lambda}-f^{*}\right\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\log^{3}(N)r_{\lambda,k}^{*}
.

Proof.
We only need to prove that
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega^{\prime\prime})>0
. This is because, as seen in Section
6.4.2
, we have seen that condition on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain the conclusion of Proposition
25
. To prove
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega^{\prime\prime})>0
, we only need to establish the left-hand side of (
101
), as the other inequalities have already been proven in Proposition
24
. The left-hand side of (
101
) is trivial, as it always holds when
\lambda\gtrsim\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})
(since
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\succeq 0
). We have provided the proof of this in Section
6.3.2
.

6.5
Auxiliary proofs

6.5.1
Proof of Proposition
1
and Proposition
8

Proof of Proposition
1

It follows from
[
TB23
, Theorem 4]
that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{49}}
, such that with probability at least
1-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_DMU}}}{N^{\epsilon}}-\gamma^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}

(110)

\displaystyle\leq\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)\left(1+\delta^{\prime}+C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}(1+\delta^{\prime})\left(\frac{1}{N}+\sqrt{\frac{N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{(1+\delta^{\prime})^{2}{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}}}+\frac{N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right\|_{\text{op}}}{(1+\delta^{\prime})^{2}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}+1)(1+\delta^{\prime})\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}\sqrt{N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right\|_{\text{op}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}}{1+\delta^{\prime}}N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left((C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}+1)(1+\delta^{\prime})+\frac{C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}}{2}\right)\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)+C_{\ref*{C_DMU_2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}\right)N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right\|_{\text{op}}.

This indicates that there exists an absolute constant
C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}>0
such that for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{{\cal H}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\left(\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}+\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}.

Proof of Proposition
8

Apply Lemma
10
below to
{\cal F}=B_{{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}}
. Then
\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}}=\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{1/2}
, and by Lemma
11
below applied to
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}
is the identity operator (recall the definition of
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}
in Proposition
3
), and
R=1
,
\left\|\gamma_{2}\left(P_{\sigma}B_{{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}},\ell_{\infty}\right)\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}Q\left(B_{{\cal H}_{k+1:\infty}}\right)\log{N}
. By Assumption
9
,
Q(B_{\cal H})\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}
. Therefore, there exist absolute constants
C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}},
and
C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}
, such that for any
\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}
with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}&={\underset{\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq 1}{\sup}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f^{2}(X_{i})}\\
&\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}^{-1}N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\\
&+\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}^{-1}C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{1/2}\sqrt{N\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\log{N}+\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}^{-1}\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\log^{2}{N}\\
&\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}^{-1}C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}^{2}\left({N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}}+{\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\log^{2}{N}}\right).\end{aligned}

(111)

When
N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\log^{2}(N)
, with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_1}}}
, for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky}}^{-1/2}C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}\log{N}\sqrt{\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_infty_1}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}.

Similarly, we have: with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_2}}-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_3}}-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_2}}}-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_3}}}
, for any
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_2}}^{-1/2}C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}\left(\sqrt{N}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}+\log{N}\sqrt{\delta_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_2}}\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2}\right)}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_upper_dvoretzky_3}}^{-1/2}C_{\ref*{C_DMU_pre}}\left(\log(N)\sqrt{\delta_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_infty_3}}}\sqrt{\square^{2}\left|J_{1}\right|+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}+\sqrt{N}\sigma(\square,\triangle)\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}.

6.5.2
Proof of Proposition
2
and Proposition
3

By homogeneity, it suffices to prove the same result for all
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
such that
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}=1
, that is, to prove that
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}\leq(1/N)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}g\right)^{2}(X_{i})\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}^{2}
where
g=\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f
for any
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
such that
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}=1
.

Proof.
We separate the proof into two parts: the upper bound and the lower bound.

Upper bound of Proposition
2

For the upper bound of RIP, we employ Proposition
1
applied to

\left[\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{1})|\cdots|\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{N})\right].

Notice that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}
have the same operator norm. By Proposition
1
,
