\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup\left(\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{\cal H}:\,\left\|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}=1\right)\leq C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}\left(\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{N}\right)\right)\geq 1-{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_RIP}}}-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_DMU}}}{N^{\epsilon}}.

As a result, with the same probability,
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}=\sup\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}g\right)^{2}(X_{i}):\,\left\|g\right\|_{\cal H}=1\right)\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}^{2}\left(k+N\right)
, which is precisely the upper bound of Proposition
2
with
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}=\sqrt{2C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}^{2}(1+c_{\ref*{c_RIP}})}
.

Lower bound of Proposition
2

For the lower bound, we use the following lemma taken from
[
Zhi24
, Proposition 4]
:

Lemma 9
.

Assume that
M_{1},\cdots,M_{N}
are independent copies of a positive semi-definite symmetric random matrix
M
with mean
{\mathbb{E}}M=\Sigma
. Let
M
satisfy that for some
\kappa\geq 1
,
\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}({\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}M{\boldsymbol{v}})^{2}}\leq\kappa^{2}{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}\Sigma{\boldsymbol{v}}
for all
{\boldsymbol{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Then for any
t>\log{2}
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t)
,

\displaystyle\inf\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\top}M_{i}{\boldsymbol{v}}-\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\geq-7\kappa^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\frac{\Tr\left(\Sigma\right)}{\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{\text{op}}}+t}{N}}.

As we can embed
\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}{\cal H}_{1:k}
into
\ell_{2}^{k}
, we apply Lemma
9
to
\Sigma=I_{k}
,
M_{i}=\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})\otimes\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X_{i})
,
\kappa=\kappa^{\prime\prime}
. By Assumption
3
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t)
, for any
g\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
such that
\left\|g\right\|_{\cal H}=1
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}g\right)^{2}(X_{i})\geq N-7(\kappa^{\prime\prime})^{2}\sqrt{N}\sqrt{k+t}
. Set
t=k
, if
c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}
is taken such that
7\sqrt{2}(\kappa^{\prime\prime})^{2}\sqrt{c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}}\leq 3/4
, that is
c_{\ref*{c_RIP}}\leq\frac{9}{1568(\kappa^{\prime\prime})^{4}}
.

In summary, Proposition
2
is verified with
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}=1/2
,
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}=\sqrt{2C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}^{2}(1+c_{\ref*{c_RIP}})}
and
\bar{p}_{RIP}={\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_RIP}}}+\frac{c_{\ref*{c_P_DMU}}}{N^{\epsilon}}+2\exp(-k)
.

Proof of Proposition
3

We first introduce a notation that will be used only in this paragraph. Let
(T,d)
be a metric space and
B\subset T
. For every
\alpha>0
, we define the Talagrand’s
\gamma_{\alpha}
-functional as

\displaystyle\gamma_{\alpha}\left(B,d\right):=\underset{({\cal A}_{n})_{n\geq 0}}{\inf}\underset{{\boldsymbol{v}}\in B}{\sup}\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{n/\alpha}d\left({\boldsymbol{v}},{\cal A}_{n}\right),

where the infimum is taken over all admissible sequences, that is,
({\cal A}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
is an increasing partition of
B
such that
\left|{\cal A}_{0}\right|=1
and for any
n\geq 1
,
\left|{\cal A}_{n}\right|\leq 2^{2^{n}}
, see
[
Tal21
, Chapter 2]
. Here
d({\boldsymbol{v}},{\cal A}_{n})=\inf\left(d({\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{u}}):{\boldsymbol{u}}\in{\cal A}_{n}\right)
is the usual distance between a point and a set.

The following lemma is taken from
[
GMPTJ07
, Theorem 1.2]

Lemma 10
.

There exists an absolute constant
{C_{50}}
such that the following holds. Let
(\Omega,\mu)
be a measured space,
N\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
and
(X_{i})_{i\in[N]}
be
N
i.i.d. random variables with values in
\Omega
. Let
{\cal F}
be a class of real-valued functions defined on
\Omega
. We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\underset{f\in{\cal F}}{\sup}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f^{2}(X_{i})-{\mathbb{E}}f^{2}(X)\right|\leq C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}\max\left(\underset{f\in{\cal F}}{\sup}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal F},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)}{N}},\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal F},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)}{N}\right),

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\underset{f\in{\cal F}}{\sup}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f^{2}(X_{i})}\leq C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}\max\left(\underset{f\in{\cal F}}{\sup}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}},\sqrt{\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal F},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)}{N}}\right),

where
P_{\sigma}{\cal F}=\left\{\left(f(X_{1}),\cdots,f(X_{N})\right)^{\top}:\,f\in{\cal F}\right\}
.

We apply Lemma
10
to
{\cal F}={\cal C}(R)
. By the definition of
{\cal C}(R)
, see (
15
), we have
{\mathbb{E}}f^{2}(X)=1
for any
f\in{\cal C}(R)
and thus
\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}}=1
. In order to apply Lemma
10
, we need to estimate
{\mathbb{E}}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal C}(R),\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)
.

If we let
\left\|f\right\|=\max\left(\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}},R\left\|f\right\|_{\cal H}\right)
for any
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
, one can check that there exists an ellipsoid norm
\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}\cdot\right\|_{\cal H}
, as defined in Proposition
3
, such that for any
f\in{\cal H}_{1:k}
,
\left\|f\right\|\leq\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|f\right\|
. The unit ball
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}
has principle lengths
\left(\min\left(\frac{1}{R},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}\right)\right)_{j\in[k]}
.
The relationship between
\left\|\cdot\right\|
and
{\cal C}(R)
is the unit ball of
\left\|\cdot\right\|
is
B_{\left\|\cdot\right\|}=R^{-1}B_{{\cal H}_{1:k}}\cap\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{{\cal H}_{1:k}}
which contains
{\cal C}(R)
. Furthermore,
B_{\left\|\cdot\right\|}\subset\sqrt{2}\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}
. Therefore,
\mu
-almost surely,
\gamma_{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal C}(R),\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)\leq\gamma_{2}\left(P_{\sigma}B_{\left\|\cdot\right\|},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)\leq\sqrt{2}\gamma_{2}\left(P_{\sigma}\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)
.

The following lemma is a rewrite of
[
MN10
, Theorem 4.7]
:

Lemma 11
.

There exists an absolute constant
{C_{51}}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma_{2}\left(P_{\sigma}\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\ell_{\infty}\right)\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}\left\|\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\log{N}.

The proof of this Lemma is almost identical to that found in
[
MN10
]
. For the sake of completeness, we include it here. Given two convex bodies
K,L\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
, we denote the covering number of
K
by
L
as
N(K,L)
, see
[
Ver18
, section 4.2]
.

Proof of Lemma
11

Notice that with probability
1
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\left\|\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}.

(112)

Denote
Q(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})
as
\left\|\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}
.
Set
\left\|f\right\|_{E}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left|f(X_{i})\right|
, and let
B_{E}
be the unit ball of
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{E}
. Recall that
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\min\{\frac{1}{R},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}}\}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}
, then for any
i\in[k]
,
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\varphi_{i}=\min\{\frac{1}{R},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}}\}\varphi_{i}
. For every
\epsilon>0
, the covering number of
\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}
by
\epsilon B_{E}
satisfies

\displaystyle N(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\epsilon B_{E})=N(B_{\cal H},\epsilon\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}B_{E}),

and
f\in\epsilon\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{1/2}B_{E}
if and only if
\max_{j\in[N]}\left|\left\langle f,\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|\leq\epsilon
. Set
\psi(X_{i})=\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})
, and
\left\|f\right\|_{\bar{E}}=\max_{j\in[N]}\left|\left\langle f,\psi(X_{i})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|
with corresponding unit ball
B_{\bar{E}}
. Then
N(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\epsilon B_{E})=N(B_{\cal H},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})=N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})
, where
B_{\cal H}^{N}
is the unit ball in the subspace of
{\cal H}
spanned by
\psi(X_{i})
’s. Let
G\in{\cal H}
be a standard Gaussian random vector, as
B_{\bar{E}}
is a convex body in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,
{\mathbb{E}}\left\|G\right\|_{\bar{E}}={\mathbb{E}}\max_{j\in[N]}\left|\left\langle G,\psi(X_{i})\right\rangle_{\cal H}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\log{N}}\max_{i\in[N]}\left\|\psi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}
, see, for example,
[
Ver18
, Exercise 7.5.10]
. Recall that
\psi(X_{i})=\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi(X_{i})
, thus from (
112
), we know that
{\mathbb{E}}\left\|G\right\|_{\bar{E}}\lesssim\sqrt{\log{N}}Q(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})
. By Sudakov’s inequality, see, for example,
[
Ver18
, Theorem 7.4.1]
,
\log N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})\lesssim\log(N)Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})/\epsilon^{2}
. In particular, the diameter of
B_{\cal H}^{N}
with respect to the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\bar{E}}
is at most
Q(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\sqrt{\log{N}}
(up to universal constant), and we denote this diameter by
D_{2}
. For small
\epsilon
, we use volumetric estimate
[
AAGM15
, Corollary 4.1.15]
. For any norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{X}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
with unit ball
B_{X}
and every
\epsilon>0
,
N(B_{X},\epsilon B_{X})\leq(3/\epsilon)^{N}
. We see that
B_{\cal H}^{N}
and
B_{\bar{E}}
are convex bodies in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
(up to isometric), thus there exists an absolute constant
{c_{17}}
such that for any
0<\epsilon<\delta
,

\displaystyle\log N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})

\displaystyle\leq\log N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\delta B_{\bar{E}})+\log N(\delta B_{\bar{E}},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})

\displaystyle\leq c_{\ref*{c_volume_estimate}}\frac{Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N)}{\delta^{2}}+N\log\left(\frac{3\delta}{\epsilon}\right).

Take
\delta^{2}=c_{\ref*{c_volume_estimate}}Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\frac{\log{N}}{N}
. There then exists an absolute constant
{c_{18}}
such that
\epsilon\leq c_{\ref*{c_upper_eps}}Q(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\sqrt{\log(N)/N}=:\epsilon_{0}
, and
\log N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})\lesssim N\log(\epsilon_{0}/\epsilon)
. By Dudley’s integral, see, for example,
[
Ver18
, Exercise 8.5.7]
, condition on the event such that (
112
) holds,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\ell_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\epsilon\log N(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\epsilon B_{E})d\epsilon=\int_{0}^{\infty}\epsilon\log N(B_{\cal H}^{N},\epsilon B_{\bar{E}})d\epsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}N\epsilon\log\left(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)d\epsilon+\int_{\epsilon_{0}}^{D_{2}}\frac{Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N)}{\epsilon}d\epsilon.

Using the change of variables for
\eta=\epsilon_{0}/\epsilon
, there exist absolute constants
{C_{52}}
and
{C_{53}}
such that the first integral is bounded by
C_{\ref*{C_upper_first_integral_1}}N\epsilon_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\eta\log(\eta^{-1})d\eta\leq C_{\ref*{C_upper_first_integral_2}}Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N)
. For the second integral, notice that there exists an absolute constant
{c_{19}}
such that
\epsilon_{0}=c_{\ref*{c_eps_0}}D_{2}/\sqrt{N}
, so the second integral is bounded from above by

\displaystyle C_{\ref*{C_upper_first_integral_2}}Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N)\left(\log(D_{2})-\log(\epsilon_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=C_{\ref*{C_upper_first_integral_2}}Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N)\left(\frac{1}{2}\log(N)-\log(c_{\ref*{c_eps_0}})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim Q^{2}(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log^{2}(N).

By Fubini’s theorem, there exists an absolute constant
C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}
such that
\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H},\ell_{\infty}\right)\right)}\leq C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}Q(\bar{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H})\log(N).

By Lemma
11
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left(P_{\sigma}{\cal C}(R),\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\ell_{\infty}}\right)\leq 2C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}^{2}Q^{2}\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H}\right)\log^{2}{N}
. Recall the definition of
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
from (
14
), for
R>R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
, by Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
, for any
0<\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}<1
, by Markov’s inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}
, for any
f\in{\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))
,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}^{2}:=1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}^{-1}\sqrt{2c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}}C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f^{2}(X_{i})\leq 1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}^{-1}\sqrt{2c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}}C_{\ref*{C_Rudelson}}C_{\ref*{C_estimate_gamma_infty}}=:C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}^{2}.

Therefore, Proposition
3
is valid with probability at least
1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_P_RIP}}
.

6.5.3
Proof of Proposition
7

The main approach for this proof is to make use of Theorem
2
. To achieve this, we need to verify Assumption
1
and check the Dvoretzky-Milman condition. As in Section
4.1
, the
L_{8}-L_{2}
equivalence is verified with
\kappa\sim 2^{L}
. We will now proceed to verify the Dvoretzky-Milman condition. As in Section
4.1
, since
L<\infty
but
d\to\infty
, we let
k=\sum_{0\leq i\leq\iota}d^{i}
, thus
k\sim d^{\iota}
, and
\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\sim 1
,
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}\sim d^{-(\iota+1)}
. As a result,
N\lesssim\kappa_{DM}d_{0}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)
.

For (
6
), since
h\in C^{\infty}
, for every
X_{i}
, there exists
\xi_{i,i}\in(1,\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d)
or
(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d,1)
such that
h\left(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d\right)=\left\|\phi(X_{i})\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}=h(1)+h^{\prime}(\xi_{i,i})\left(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d-1\right)
.
By
[
Ver18
, Theorem 3.1.1]
together with a union bound over
i\in[N]
, there exists an absolute constant
c_{0}>0
such that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\underset{i\in[N]}{\max}\left|\frac{\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}-1\right|\geq t\right)\leq N\exp\left(-c_{0}\frac{t^{2}d}{\left\|x_{1}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}}\right).

Let
t=1/\sqrt{\log\log{N}}
. We have
\lim_{N\to\infty}t=0
, and
\lim_{N,d\to\infty}N\exp\left(-c_{0}(t^{2}d)/\left\|x_{1}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\right)\to 0
. This indicates that
\max_{i\in[N]}\left|\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d-1\right|\to 0
in probability as
N,d\to\infty
. Moreover, as
h\in C^{\infty}
,
h^{\prime}(\xi_{i,i})
is bounded in a neighborhood of
1
uniformly over
i\in[N]
. These observations imply that
h(\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d)\to h(1)
in probability uniformly over
i\in[N]
as
N,d\to\infty
. Consequently, we may take
\delta=\gamma=0
in (
6
).

Now that we have checked all the conditions of Theorem
2
, we can apply this theorem. The conclusion of Proposition
7
is a straightforward consequence of Theorem
2
.

6.5.4
Estimating the fixed point of RIP

In the following, we estimate
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
in some cases.
We now separate into three regimes depending the following condition: For some absolute constant
\theta_{1}
such that
0\leq{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}\leq 1
and an absolute constant
{C_{54}}
such that for any
j\in[k]
,
\left\|f_{j}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}\left\|f_{j}\right\|_{\cal H}^{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}\left\|f_{j}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}
. This condition is widely used in RKHS literature, for instance,
[
MN10
,
SHS09
,
FS20
,
LZL23
]
.

{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}=0

In this case,

\displaystyle R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})=\inf\left\{R>0:\,\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}\wedge R^{2}}\leq c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}R\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\log{N}}\right\}.

Therefore, up to a logarithmic factor, we recover the fixed point in the sub-Gaussian case. More precisely,

1.

When
k\leq\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log^{2}(N)}
. Then
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})=0
.

2.

When there exists
k_{0}\in\left[\lfloor\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\rfloor N\right]
such that
\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\sigma_{j}\leq\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log{N}}-k_{0}+1\right)\sigma_{k_{0}}
, let

\displaystyle k^{**}=\max\left(k_{0}\in\left[\lfloor\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\rfloor N\right]:\,\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\sigma_{j}\leq\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log{N}}-k_{0}+1\right)\sigma_{k_{0}}\right),

then
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\leq\sigma_{k^{**}}
.

3.

When for all
k_{0}\in\left[\lfloor\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\rfloor N\right]
, we have
\sum_{j\geq k_{0}}\sigma_{j}>\left(\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log{N}}-k_{0}+1\right)\sigma_{k_{0}}
. Then
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}}{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}}{\frac{\sqrt{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{1:k}\right)}\log{N}}{\sqrt{N}}}
.

0<{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}<1

We have

\displaystyle Q\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H}\right)\leq\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}{R^{2}}\wedge\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}}.

(113)

The estimation of
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
in this case is somewhat complicated, we thus only provide two trivial estimates:

1.

If
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}\leq\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log^{2}(N)}
, then
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})=0
.

2.

We always have
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\leq\sqrt{\frac{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}{N}}\log{N}
.

As an example, when
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
, one may take
{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}=\alpha^{-1}
, see
[
SHS09
, Corollary 3]
, see also
[
MN10
, Lemma 5.1]
.

1.

When
1<\alpha<2
, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}\sim k^{-\alpha}
. Hence
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k^{-\alpha}}{N}}\log{N}
.

2.

When
\alpha=2
, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}\sim\log{k}
. Hence
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\lesssim\frac{\log(N)\sqrt{\log(k)}}{\sqrt{N}}
.

3.

When
\alpha>2
, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}\sim 1
. Hence
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\lesssim\frac{\log(N)}{\sqrt{N}}
.

Apart from the above estimate, when
\alpha>2
, if there exists
k_{0}<k
such that
\sum_{j=k_{0}+1}^{k}\frac{j^{1-\alpha}}{R^{2}}\lesssim\frac{N}{\log^{2}(N)}-\sum_{j=1}^{k_{0}}j
, we can set
R_{N}^{2}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\lesssim\frac{k_{0}^{2-\alpha}}{\frac{N}{\log^{2}{N}}-k_{0}^{2}}
.

{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}=1

We have
Q\left(\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\cap RB_{\cal H}\right)\leq\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{R^{2}}\wedge\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}}.
The estimation of
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})
is also difficult in this case. We provide two trivial estimates.

1.

When
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{-1}\leq\frac{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}^{2}}{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}\frac{N}{\log^{2}{N}}
,
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})=0
.

2.

We always have
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\ref*{C_embedding}}}}{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}}\frac{\log{N}\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{N}}
.

6.5.5
Proof of Proposition
5

By (
5
), we have:

\displaystyle d_{\lambda}^{*}\left(\Gamma_{k+1:2k}^{-1/2}B_{\cal H}\right)\gtrsim\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)+\lambda}{\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\sim\frac{k^{1-\alpha}+N^{\frac{-\alpha}{1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha}+1}}{(k+1)^{-\alpha}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\alpha}N^{\frac{\alpha}{1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha}}N^{\frac{-\alpha}{1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha}+1}\geq\frac{N}{c_{\ref*{c_kappa_DM}}\kappa_{DM}}.

By (
3
), we know that
J_{1}=[k]
and
J_{2}=\emptyset
, hence
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:\mathrm{thre}}=\Gamma_{1:k}
. Moreover,
k^{\frac{1}{\log{d}}}=N^{\frac{1}{(1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha)\log{d}}}\lesssim\frac{N}{\log^{8}(N)}
and by (
113
) and
{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}=\frac{1}{\alpha}
, we check that we can take
R\to 0
, that is, there exists an absolute constant
{C_{55}}
such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{\sigma_{j}^{1-{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}{R^{2}}\wedge\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{{\theta_{\ref*{theta_embedding}}}}}}\leq C_{\ref*{C_RIP_equation_1}}k\sim N^{\frac{1}{1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha}}<c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\log^{8}(N)},

where we have used the fact that for any
j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
and
s\alpha>1
. Hence we can take
R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}})=0
, and thus by (
15
),
\mathrm{cone}\left({\cal C}(R_{N}(c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}))\right)={\cal H}_{1:k}
. Up to now, we have checked all the assumptions in Proposition
13
. We now compute the terms appeared in the upper bound in Proposition
13
.

1.

\sigma_{\xi}\frac{\sqrt{Nk^{1-2\alpha}}}{\lambda+k^{1-\alpha}}\lesssim\sigma_{\xi}N^{-\frac{(s\wedge 2)\alpha}{2(1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha)}}
,

2.

\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\left|J\right|}{N}}\lesssim\sigma_{\xi}N^{-\frac{(s\wedge 2)\alpha}{2(1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha)}}
.

3.

We compute the bias term (referred to the terms without the
\sigma_{\xi}
factor) at once. We first consider the case when
0<s<2
.

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|\Gamma_{1,k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\frac{\lambda^{2}+k^{2(1-\alpha)}}{N^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}a_{j}^{2}+N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+s\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{-1}a_{j}^{2}.

(114)

Recall that
\sigma_{j}\sim j^{-\alpha}
is a decreasing function of
j
. When
j>k
, by
\sigma_{j}^{2}\vee\sigma_{k}^{2}=\sigma_{k}^{2}
, we have:
\sigma_{j}\leq\frac{2\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}\sigma_{j}
; When
j\leq k
, we know that
\sigma_{j}^{2}\vee\sigma_{k}^{2}=\sigma_{j}^{2}
, hence
\sigma_{j}^{-1}\leq\frac{2\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}
. Plugging these facts into (
114
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}a_{j}^{2}+N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+s\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{-1}a_{j}^{2}\leq 2\sigma_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\frac{a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}+2N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+s\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}.

Recall that by our choice of
k
,
\sigma_{k}^{2}\sim N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+s\alpha}}
, we further derive that

\displaystyle 2\sigma_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\frac{a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}+2N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+s\alpha}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}\lesssim\sigma_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\frac{\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{k}^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{\frac{s}{2}}\sigma_{k}}{\sigma_{j}+\sigma_{k}}\right)^{2}.

(115)

For
0<s<2
, let
g(x)=\frac{\lambda x^{s/2}}{\lambda+x}
. Then
\sup_{x>0}g(x)=\frac{2-s}{2}\left(\frac{s}{s-2}\right)^{s/2}\lambda^{s/2}
. Let
x=\sigma_{j}
and
\lambda=\sigma_{k}
. Then (
115
) together with (
28
) indicate that there exists an absolute constant
{C_{56}}
depending only on
s
such that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:bias_smooth_compute_2}<C_{\ref*{C_bias_smooth_compute_1}}\sigma_{k}^{s}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}\leq C_{\ref*{C_bias_smooth_compute_1}}\sigma_{k}^{s}\sim N^{-\frac{\alpha s}{1+\alpha s}}.

4.

When
s\geq 2
, recall that the choice of
k=N^{\frac{1}{1+2\alpha}}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}=\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}a_{j}^{2}=\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}^{s}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}a_{j}^{2}<\sigma_{k+1}^{s}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}a_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\alpha s}=N^{-\frac{\alpha s}{1+2\alpha}}\leq N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}+\Tr^{2}(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N^{2}}\left\|\Gamma_{1,k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\lesssim\sigma_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{-1}a_{j}^{2}=\sigma_{k}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}a_{j}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{s-2}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{k}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{s-2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{j}^{1-s}a_{j}^{2}\lesssim N^{-\frac{2\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}.

Combining the above three terms together with Proposition
13
, we know that with constant probability,

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{f}_{\lambda}-f^{*}\right\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\log^{3}(N)N^{-\frac{\alpha(s\wedge 2)}{2(1+(s\wedge 2)\alpha)}}.

6.5.6
Proof of Proposition
4

By the choice of
k
we obtain
\sigma_{k+1}\lesssim\frac{\lambda}{N}\lesssim\sigma_{k}
, and
\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})+\lambda\gtrsim N\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right\|_{\text{op}}
and we have Dvoretzky-Milman theorem.

1.

The bias term. We first consider the case when
f^{*}\in{\cal H}
.
By the choice of
k
,
\sigma_{j}\vee\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N}\gtrsim\sigma_{j}
for any
j\leq k
.
We have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N}\lesssim\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\left(\frac{\lambda}{N\sqrt{\sigma_{k}}}+\frac{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N\sqrt{\sigma_{k}}}\right)

Recall that we have chosen
k
, such that
\sigma_{k}\gtrsim\frac{\lambda}{N}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\Gamma}_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{N}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{N}}\left(1+\frac{\Tr(\Gamma)}{\lambda}\right)\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda}{N}+\frac{\Tr^{2}(\Gamma)}{N\lambda}\right)\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}\left(\Gamma+\frac{\lambda}{N}I\right)^{-1/2}f^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}

\displaystyle\sim\left(\frac{\lambda}{N}+\frac{\Tr^{2}(\Gamma)}{N\lambda}\right)\left(\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\frac{N}{\lambda}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\lambda}{N}\left(1+\frac{\Tr^{2}(\Gamma)}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\left\|f_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}+\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}.

The case when
f^{*}
may not belong to
{\cal H}
follows directly from
[
Bac24
, Lemma 7.2, Equation 7.20 and Equation 7.21]
(notice that their
\lambda
corresponds to our
\lambda/N
).

2.

Variance term.
By the definition of
\Gamma(\Gamma+(\lambda/N)I)^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}\left(\Gamma(\Gamma+(\lambda/N)I)^{-1}\right)\sim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1,&j\leq k\\
\frac{N\sigma_{j}}{\lambda},&j\geq k+1.\end{cases}

Then

\displaystyle\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\left(\Gamma+\frac{\lambda}{N}I\right)^{-1}\right)}{N}\sim\frac{k}{N}+\frac{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{\lambda}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{k}{N}}\lesssim\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma\left(\Gamma+\frac{\lambda}{N}I\right)^{-1}\right)}{N}}.

Moreover, recall that
\sigma_{k+1}\lesssim\frac{\lambda}{N}
, hence

\displaystyle\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}{(\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}))^{2}}\leq\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}^{2})}{\lambda^{2}}\leq\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N\sigma_{k+1}}{\lambda}\frac{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{\lambda}\lesssim\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})}{\lambda}.

6.5.7
Proof of Proposition
12

The proof strategy for this Proposition involves verifying the stochastic argument of Theorem
3
in the Gaussian case.

We prove (
33
) by the Gaussian Dvoretzky-Milman theorem, see
[
LS22
, Theorem 5]
, (
34
), (
70
) by the Gaussian isomorphy property and restricted isomorphy property, see
[
LS22
, Theorem 6]
, (
61
) by
[
LS22
, Proposition 5]
. (
36
) and (
37
) follow from Bernstein’s inequality. The deterministic arguments from Section
6.1
and Section
6.2
then still hold. Since the proof is exactly the same, we have omitted it here.

The variance term in the lower bound comes from
[
TB23
]
. Given that the lower bound on the bias term proved in
[
TB23
]
takes the form of a Bayesian lower bound (where they assume
f^{*}
to be a random vector), we present here a general form of the lower bound. The proof methodology is inspired by the work in
[
LS22
]
. The proof presented here only requires that the marginal distribution of
X
satisfies the equivalence of
L_{2+\epsilon}
and
L_{2}
, and that
X
is a centered symmetric random vector, where
\epsilon>2
. This encompasses the case of a Gaussian random vector as a special instance.

Stochastic Argument

The underlying principle of the stochastic arguments employed is based on the lemma presented in
[
BLLT20
, Lemma 9]
:

Lemma 12
.

Suppose
p\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}
,
\{\eta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{p}
is a sequence of non-negative random variables, and
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{p}
is a sequence of non-negative real numbers(at least one of which is strictly positive) such that, for some
0<\delta<1
and any
i\leq p
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\eta_{i}>t_{i}\right)\geq 1-\delta
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\eta_{i}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{p}t_{i}\right)\geq 1-2\delta.

The purpose of Lemma
12
is to present an alternate approach to the union bound.
Consider the case where
\phi:\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, specifically for the scenario of ridge regression. In this context, the bias term of the lower bound involves
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}
, which is equivalent to
{\mathbb{X}}P_{k+1:\infty}
. Here, we employ
P_{k+1:\infty}
to accommodate the possibility that
d
may be infinite. That is to say, when
j>d
, we straightforwardly set
\varphi_{j}=\mathbf{0}
(zero vector) and
\sigma_{j}=0
. In this case, we view
{\cal H}
as
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
by viewing
f\in{\cal H}
as some
{\boldsymbol{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, that is,
f(\cdot)=\left<{\boldsymbol{v}},\cdot\right>
, hence
\left<\phi({\boldsymbol{x}}),f\right>_{\cal H}=f({\boldsymbol{x}})=\left<{\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{x}}\right>
.

Let
{c_{20}}
,
{c_{21}}
,
{C_{57}}
,
{C_{58}}
be absolute constants, where
c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}
depends on
\kappa
.
For each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define
\Omega_{j}
as the random event on which:

•

{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}
is well-conditioned, that is,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_lower_1}}\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)\leq\sigma_{N}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right).

(116)

•

Let
z_{j}=(1/\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}){\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\varphi_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
, and
Q
be a (random) projection matrix that is independent with
z_{j}
. Condition on
Q
,

\displaystyle z_{j}^{\top}Qz_{j}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}\Tr(Q)+C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}N\left\|Q\right\|_{op}.

(117)

•

For
z_{j}
defined as above,

\displaystyle c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}N\leq\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}N.

(118)

We emphasize that all the absolute constants
c_{\ref*{c_lower_1}}
,
C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}
are independent with
j
.

In the subsequent portion of this paragraph, we establish the inequality
2{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Omega_{j}\right)\geq 99/100
for every
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, assuming the conditions of the Dvoretzky-Milman theorem are satisfied. Because
b>C_{\ref*{C_lower_kappa_DM}}/\kappa_{DM}
, (
116
) is satisfied with a probability of at least
1-\bar{p}_{DM}
, hence our focus now shifts to establishing the validity of (
117
) and (
118
) with a high level of probability.

Lemma 13
.

For each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define
z_{j}=(1/\sqrt{\sigma_{j}}){\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\varphi_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
. Let
Q
be a (random) projection matrix that is independent with
z_{j}
. Then there exists an absolute constant
{c_{22}}<10^{-3}
such that condition on
Q
, for each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_1}}
,

z_{j}^{\top}Qz_{j}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}\Tr(Q)+C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}N\left\|Q\right\|_{op}.

Proof.
Recall that for each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}\varphi_{j}=\left(\varphi_{j}(X_{i})\right)_{i\in[N]}
, thus
z_{j}
has i.i.d. coordinates
(z_{ji})_{i\in[N]}
. Furthermore, for each
i\in[N]
,
\left\|\varphi_{j}(X_{i})\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}=\sigma_{j}
, thus
\left\|z_{ji}\right\|_{L_{2}}=1
. We write
{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}=({\mathbb{E}}z_{ji})_{i\in[N]}
. By the linearity,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}^{\top}Qz_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}+{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}+{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}+{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}+{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}[z_{j1}^{2}]\Tr(Q)+2{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{j}-{\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})\right]+({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}[z_{j1}^{2}]\Tr(Q)+({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})^{\top}Q({\mathbb{E}}z_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Tr(Q)+\left\|Q\right\|_{op}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\Tr(Q)+N\left\|Q\right\|_{op}.

By Markov’s inequality, there exists an absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_1}}<10^{-3}
such that with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_1}}
,

z_{j}^{\top}Qz_{j}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}\Tr(Q)+C_{\ref*{C_lower_2}}N\left\|Q\right\|_{op}.

Lemma 14
.

Grant the following assumption: there exists an constant
\kappa\geq 1
such that for any
f\in{\cal H}
, we have
\left\|f\right\|_{L_{4}}\leq\kappa\left\|f\right\|_{L_{2}}
. Then there exist absolute constants
{c_{23}}
,
{c_{24}}
,
c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}
where
c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}
and
c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_2}}
depend on
\kappa
, such that
c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}<10^{-3}
and with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}-\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_2}}N)
,

c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}N\leq\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}N.

Proof.
{\mathbb{E}}\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}=N
. Therefore there exists an absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}<10^{-3}
such that by Markov’s inequality, with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}
,

\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}^{-1}N.

For the lower side, we use Paley-Zygmund inequality(see, for example,
[
dlPG99
, section 3.3]
) together with a Bernstein’s inequality(see, for example,
[
Ver18
, section 2.8]
) for selectors. Hence there exists an absolute constant
{c_{25}}
depending on
\kappa
such that for one
i\in[N]
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(z_{ji}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4}\frac{\left({\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{2}\right)^{2}}{{\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{4}}\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_3}},

where we used
{\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{4}=(1/\sigma_{j}^{2}){\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{j}^{4}(X_{i})\leq\kappa^{4}(1/\sigma_{j}^{2})\left({\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{j}^{2}(X_{i})\right)^{2}=\kappa^{4}\left({\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{2}\right)^{2}
since
\varphi_{j}\in{\cal H}
.

By Bernstein’s inequality for
(\delta_{i})_{i\in[N]}
, where
\delta_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{ji}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{2}\}}
, there exists an absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_2}}
such that with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_2}}N)
, we have
\left|\left\{i\in[N]:\,z_{ji}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}z_{ji}^{2}\right\}\right|\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_3}}N
. Therefore, there exist absolute constants
c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}
depending on
\kappa
and
C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}
such that with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_3}}-\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_p_lower_2}}N)
,

c_{\ref*{c_lower_2}}N\leq\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\ref*{C_lower_3}}N.

Deterministic Argument

Proposition 26
.

Assume that
\phi:{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mapsto{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
X
is centered and symmetric with covariance matrix
\Gamma
, and satisfies Assumption
1
with
\epsilon>2
. Moreover, assume that
Z=\Gamma^{-1/2}X
is a random vector with independent coordinates. There exist absolute constants
b
,
{c_{26}}
,
{c_{27}}
,
{C_{59}}
, where
c_{\ref*{c_lower_5}}
depends on
b
and
b
depends on
\kappa_{DM}
, such that if
N\geq C_{\ref*{C_lower_4}}
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}-I\right)f^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_4}}\left\|\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{1/2}f_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}^{2}

\displaystyle+c_{\ref*{c_lower_5}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{-1/2}f_{1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty})}{N}\right)^{2}.

Proof.
Let
U=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}:{\cal H}\to{\cal H}
where
(\epsilon_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables. Then

\displaystyle UU^{\top}=\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}\right)\left(\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{i}\varphi_{i}\otimes\varphi_{i}\right)=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}=I=U^{\top}U

implies that
U
is a unitary operator, and the absolute convergence of
\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}
implies that

\displaystyle U\Gamma

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}\right)\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\sigma_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}\right)=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\sigma_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\epsilon_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}\right)\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\sigma_{j}\varphi_{j}\otimes\varphi_{j}\right)=\Gamma U,

thus
U
commutes with
\Gamma
. As
\phi:{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mapsto{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
X
is symmetric, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I)Uf^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}\left(({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}U)^{\top}\left(({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}U)({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}U)^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}U-I\right)f^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I\right)f^{*}\right\|_{{\cal H}}.

In the following, we obtain lower bound for
{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I)Uf^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}
.
For all
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, denote
f_{j}^{*}:=\left<f^{*},\varphi_{j}\right>_{{\cal H}}
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{U}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I)Uf^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\left\|\Gamma^{1/2}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I)\varphi_{j}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\left<\varphi_{j},\Gamma^{1/2}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}-I)\varphi_{j}\right>_{\cal H}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\sigma_{j}\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}.

Let
j\geq k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1
. On
\Omega_{j}
, there exist absolute constants
{C_{60}}
and
{C_{61}}
that do not depend on
j
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}\right\|_{op}\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\ref*{C_lower_6}}\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty})}}\leq\frac{C_{\ref*{C_lower_7}}\sqrt{N}}{\sqrt{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty})}},

where we used that (by (
116
))

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}={\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{\top}+{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\succeq{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}

\displaystyle\succeq c_{\ref*{c_lower_1}}\left(\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)\right)I_{N}.

Therefore, there exist absolute constants
{c_{28}}
and
{c_{29}}
such that if
b>2c_{\ref*{c_lower_7}}^{-1}
such that

\displaystyle\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\geq 1-2\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_lower_6}}\sigma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1}N}{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty})}>1-\frac{c_{\ref*{c_lower_7}}}{b}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Therefore, by Lemma
12
, there exists an absolute constant
c_{\ref*{c_lower_4}}
such that

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}}\left[\sum_{j>k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{\infty}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\sigma_{j}\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_4}}\left\|\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{1/2}f^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}.

Now, let
1\leq j\leq k_{b,\lambda}^{*}
, define
A=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\sigma_{j}z_{j}\otimes z_{j}+\lambda I_{N}
, and
A_{-i}=A-\sigma_{i}z_{i}\otimes z_{i}
. By Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury formula

\sigma_{j}\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{j}}{\left(1+\sigma_{j}z_{j}^{\top}A_{-j}^{-1}z_{j}\right)^{2}}.

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,
z_{j}^{\top}A_{-j}^{-1}z_{j}\leq\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}\left\|A_{-j}^{-1}z_{j}\right\|\leq\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|A_{-j}\right\|_{op}=\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}/s_{N}(A_{-j})
, where
s_{N}(A_{-j})
is the
N
-th largest singular value of
A_{-j}
. Since
j\leq k_{b,\lambda}^{*}
,
A_{-j}\succeq{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}
. Condition on
\Omega_{j}
, there exists an absolute constant
{C_{62}}
that is independent with
j
such that

\displaystyle z_{j}^{\top}A_{-j}^{-1}z_{j}\leq\frac{\left\|z_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{s_{N}(A_{-j})}\leq\frac{C_{\ref*{C_lower_8}}N}{\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)}

Further, since by definition of
k_{b,\lambda}^{*}
and since
j\leq k_{b,\lambda}^{*}
,
bN\sigma_{j}>\Tr(\Gamma_{j:\infty})+\lambda\geq\Tr(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty})+\lambda
for
j\leq k_{b,\lambda}^{*}
. Therefore, there exist absolute constants
{C_{63}}
and
{C_{64}}
depend on
b
such that

\frac{\sigma_{j}}{\left(1+\sigma_{j}z_{j}^{\top}A_{-j}^{-1}z_{j}\right)^{2}}\geq\frac{\sigma_{j}}{\left(\frac{C_{\ref*{C_lower_9}}\sigma_{j}N}{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)}\right)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_lower_10}}}\frac{\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)+\lambda\right)^{2}}{\sigma_{j}N^{2}}.

As we have
2{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Omega_{j}\right)>99/100
and by Lemma
12
, there exists an absolute constant
{c_{30}}
depending on
b
such that with probability at least
9/10
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j>k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{\infty}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\sigma_{j}\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j>k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_lower_10}}}\frac{\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)+\lambda\right)^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{*}}{\sigma_{j}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_8}}\frac{\left(\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)+\lambda\right)^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{*}}{\sigma_{j}}\right)^{2}.

As a result,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}}\left[\sum_{j>k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{\infty}(f_{j}^{*})^{2}\sigma_{j}\left(1-\sigma_{j}\left\|\left({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N}\right)^{-1/2}z_{j}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq c_{\ref*{c_lower_5}}\left\|\Gamma_{1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}^{-1}f_{1:k_{b,\lambda}^{*}}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}\frac{\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k_{b,\lambda}^{*}+1:\infty}\right)+\lambda}{N}.

Proposition
6
follows from Proposition
26
and the lower bound of the variance term from
[
TB23
]
.

6.5.8
Proof of Proposition
10

In order to achieve a more accurate probability deviation, we will proceed to provide a new stochastic argument for the proof of Theorem

and Theorem

. Since this section focuses only on the estimation error of KRR defined by the data-dependent conjugate kernel, we replace
N_{2}
with
N
.

Proof of diagonal concentration assumption

The following lemma is taken from
[
WZ23
, Corollary 3.5]
, with their
{\boldsymbol{w}}
replaced by our design vector
X
, their matrix
X
replaced by
{\mathbb{W}}^{\top}
defined in the following lemma.

Lemma 15

(
[
WZ23
]
)
.

Let
X\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be a Gaussian random vector,
{\mathbb{W}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
and
A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times m}
be deterministic matrices. Define
\boldsymbol{\phi}=\sigma({\mathbb{W}}X)
a random vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. Suppose
\sigma
is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant
\lambda_{\sigma}>0
. There exists an absolute constant
{C_{65}}
such that for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\boldsymbol{\phi}^{\top}A\boldsymbol{\phi}-\Tr\left(A{\mathbb{E}}\left[\boldsymbol{\phi}\otimes\boldsymbol{\phi}\right]\right)\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_wang_deformed}}}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{8\lambda_{\sigma}^{4}\left\|{\mathbb{W}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{4}\left\|A\right\|_{HS}^{2}},\frac{t}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|{\mathbb{W}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{32\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|{\mathbb{W}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}t_{0}}\right),

where
t_{0}=2\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\left\|W_{i}\right\|_{2}-1\right)^{2}+2m\left|{\mathbb{E}}\sigma(g)\right|^{2}
,
W_{i}
is the
i
-th row of
{\mathbb{W}}
and
g
is a standard Gaussian random variable.

Let
{\mathbb{W}}=W_{J^{c}}
,
A=\frac{1}{m}I_{m}
, and
t=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}
in Lemma
15
. Take independent copies of
\boldsymbol{\phi}
as
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\phi}_{N}
. A union bound together with Lemma
15
leads to (
6
) with
\delta=1/2
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma&=2N\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_wang_deformed}}}\min\left\{\frac{m({\mathbb{E}}\left\|\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2})^{2}}{32\lambda_{\sigma}^{4}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{4}},\frac{m{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}}\right\}\right)\\
&+2N\exp\left(-\frac{m^{2}({\mathbb{E}}\left\|\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2})^{2}}{256\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}(\sum_{i\in J^{c}}\left(\left\|W_{i}\right\|_{2}-1\right)^{2}+2(m-k)\left|{\mathbb{E}}\sigma(g)\right|^{2})}\right).\end{split}

(119)

Let
{\mathbb{W}}=W_{J^{c}}
,
A=\frac{1}{m}\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}
where
\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X)\otimes\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X)\right]
and
t=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}
in Lemma

. Take independent copies of
\boldsymbol{\phi}
as
\boldsymbol{\phi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\phi}_{N}
. A union bound together with Lemma
15
implies (
12
) with
\delta_{\ref*{delta_DMU_L2}}=1/2
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}&=2N\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C_{\ref*{C_wang_deformed}}}\min\left\{\frac{m^{2}\left({\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}}{32\lambda_{\sigma}^{4}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{4}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right\|_{HS}^{2}},\frac{m{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}}{2\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right\|_{\text{op}}}\right\}\right)\\
&+2N\exp\left(-\frac{m^{2}\left({\mathbb{E}}\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{1/2}\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}}{256\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}(\sum_{i\in J^{c}}(\left\|W_{j}\right\|_{2}-1)^{2}+2(m-k)\left|{\mathbb{E}}\sigma(g)\right|^{2})\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(120)

Proof of (
33
)

By
[
WZ23
, Equation 5.2]
,
(\Gamma_{ij})=\frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\left<W_{i},G\right>)\sigma\left<W_{j},G\right>]=\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}
for any
i,j\in J^{c}
, since
\left<W_{i},W_{j}\right>=\delta_{ij}
by Assumption
6
, item
3
. Then for any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\cap\ell_{2}^{J^{c}}
where
\ell_{2}^{J^{c}}=\Span({\boldsymbol{e}}_{j}:j\in J^{c})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\phi_{k+1:m}(G)\right>\right\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in J^{c}}a_{i}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\left<W_{i},G\right>)]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\neq l\\
j,l\in J^{c}\end{subarray}}a_{j}a_{l}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\left<W_{j},G\right>)\sigma(\left<W_{l},G\right>)]=\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}.

On the other hand, by Borel-TIS inequality,
\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\phi_{k+1:m}(G)\right>\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\|W_{J^{c}}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}/m
. Hence

\displaystyle\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\phi_{k+1:m}(G)\right>\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}{m}\frac{\left\|W_{J^{c}}\right\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}\|W_{J^{c}}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\left\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\phi_{k+1:m}(G)\right>\right\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}.

Since
\|W_{J^{c}}\|_{\text{op}}=1
, we omit it in the following.

1.

We first deal with the case when
\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty})
dominates. By
[
Ver18
, Equation 2.14]
, there exists an absolute constant
{c_{31}}
such that for any
t>0
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|f_{{\boldsymbol{w}}}(G)\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-c_{\ref*{c_P_sub_gaussian}}t^{2}/\left\|f_{{\boldsymbol{w}}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\right)
. As a result, for any
{\boldsymbol{w}}\in S_{2}^{m-1}\cap\ell_{2}^{J^{c}}
and any
t>0
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{c_{\ref*{c_P_sub_gaussian}}}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}\left|f_{\boldsymbol{w}}(G)\right|/(\lambda_{\sigma}\sqrt{\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\|_{\text{op}}})\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)
. We have so far verified all the conditions of
[
TB23
, Theorem 4]
. Therefore we use
[
TB23
, Theorem 4]
but with the use of
[
GLPTJ17
, Theorem 2.1, case 1]
replaced by
[
GLPTJ17
, Theorem 2.1, case 2]
. Moreover, because we have assumed that

\displaystyle\frac{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})+\lambda}{\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right\|_{\text{op}}}\gtrsim\frac{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}}{\left\|\sigma\right\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}N,

which is Assumption
6
,
4.(b)
, we thus have that (
33
) holds with probability at least
1-\gamma-\sqrt{\beta}=:1-\bar{p}_{DM}
, where
\gamma
is defined in (
119
) and
\beta
is defined as

\displaystyle\beta=\frac{1}{\left(10N\right)^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{4N}{\left(3.5\log(2N)\right)^{4}}\right)+\frac{N^{2}}{2\exp\left(4N\right)}.

(121)

2.

When
\lambda
dominates, the proof idea is the same as in Section
6.3.2
. The lower side is trivial, so we omit it. The upper side follows by applying the upper bound of
A_{k}
from
[
GLPTJ17
, Theorem 2.1, case 2]
, with their
k=N
,
t=\sqrt{N}
,
\tau=1
,
\alpha=\lambda=2
, and their
X_{i}
replaced by our
\sqrt{c_{\ref*{c_P_sub_gaussian}}}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}/(\lambda_{\sigma}\sqrt{\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\|_{\text{op}}})\phi_{k+1:\infty}(X_{i})
. We obtain that with probability at least
1-\bar{p}_{DM}
(with
\bar{p}_{DM}
defined above),
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})}+\lambda_{\sigma}\sqrt{N\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\|_{\text{op}}}/(\sqrt{c_{\ref*{c_P_sub_gaussian}}}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})})
. Due to Assumption
6
,
4.(b)
, we have
\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}\right\|_{\text{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})+\lambda}
. (

) then follows.

Proof of (
34
)

The following Lemma is taken from
[
Ver11
, Theorem 5.39]
:

Lemma 16

(
[
Ver11
]
)
.

Assume
A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N\times n}
is a matrix whose rows
A_{i}
are i.i.d. sub-Gaussian isotropic random vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. There exist absolute constants
{c_{32}}
and
{C_{66}}\sim\left\|A_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}
such that for every
t\geq 0
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_P_vershynin}}t^{2}\left\|A_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{-4})
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}-C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sqrt{n}-t\leq\sigma_{\text{min}}(A)\leq\sigma_{1}(A)\leq\sqrt{N}+C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sqrt{n}+t.

Recall that we have assumed
\left\|W_{J}\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq B
,
\sum_{j\in J}\left(\left\|W_{j}\right\|_{2}^{2}-1\right)^{2}\leq B^{2}
and
\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq\lambda_{\sigma}
. As in
[
FW20
, Proof of Lemma D.4]
, we know that
\left\|\sigma(W_{J}G)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{\sigma}B
. Hence
\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\phi_{1:k}(X)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{\sigma}B\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{op}}/\sqrt{m}
thus
C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sim\frac{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}B^{2}}{m\sigma_{k}}
.
Let
A
in Lemma
16
be
{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2}
,
n=k
with
c_{\ref*{c_kappa_RIP}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\min\{1,C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}^{-2}\}
, and
t=\frac{C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}}{2}\sqrt{k}
. Then
t^{2}\left\|A_{i}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{-4}\sim k
, and
\sqrt{N}-C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sqrt{n}-t=\sqrt{N}-\frac{1}{2}C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sqrt{k}\geq\sqrt{N}-\frac{1}{2}C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}^{-2}N}=\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{N}
.
Then by Lemma
16
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_P_vershynin}}k)=:1-\bar{p}_{RIP}
. By the assumption that
2\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}B^{2}\leq m\sigma_{k}\sqrt{N}
(this is Assumption
6
, 4.(c)), we have
k\geq 1
, and thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{N}\leq\sigma_{\text{min}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2})\leq\sigma_{1}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\Gamma_{1:k}^{-1/2})\leq\frac{7}{4}\sqrt{N}.

By the homogeneity argument as in Section
6.5.2
together with the isometry of
{\cal H}_{1:k}\hookrightarrow\ell_{2}^{k}
, there exists some
{\delta_{12}}=\frac{3}{4}
such that

\displaystyle(1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_approx_isometry}})\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}f\right\|_{2}\leq(1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_approx_isometry}})\left\|\Gamma_{1:k}^{1/2}f\right\|_{\cal H}.

Therefore, we can take
\bar{p}_{RIP}=2\exp(-c_{\ref*{c_P_vershynin}}k)
,
c_{\ref*{c_RIP_lower}}=1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_approx_isometry}}
and
C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}=1+\delta_{\ref*{delta_approx_isometry}}
.

)

Therefore, by
[

, Theorem 4]
but with

So far, we have successfully reestablished the proofs for Proposition

. Since the deterministic argument remains unchanged, we can now conclude our proof.

Remark 6
.

Our Assumption
6
[1] requires the design vector
X
to be a Gaussian random vector (when
X
is anisotropic, replace the hidden layer matrix
W
with
W\Sigma^{1/2}
, where
\Sigma={\mathbb{E}}[XX^{\top}]
). When
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{d})
has independent (but not necessarily identically distributed) coordinates, we need to generalize Lemma
15
and
[
FW20
, Lemma D.4]
. The proof of Lemma
15
only utilizes the convex concentration property of
X
, as defined in
[
Ada15
, Definition 2.2]
. This property has been proved in
[
Tal96
,
KZ20
,
HT23
,
Sam23
,
AK23
]
to the case where
x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}
are Orlicz random variables satisfying the Hoffmann-Jorgensen condition, as stated in
[
AK23
, Proposition 4.7]
. Therefore, Lemma
15
still holds for a broad class of probability measures, but with a logarithmic factor in
m
as a cost, which is unavoidable (see
[
HT23
]
).
The proof of Proposition
10
also relies on showing that
\sigma(W_{J}X)
and
\sigma(W_{J^{c}}X)
are sub-Gaussian random vectors (we conjecture that sub-Weibull random vectors would be sufficient, see
[
KC22
, Section 4.2]
). We utilize
[
FW20
, Lemma D.4]
to establish this result, which in turn relies on Gaussian Poincaré inequality and Gaussian Lipschitz concentration inequality.
When
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{d})
satisfies that
x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}
are independent, and for any
i\in[d]
,
\|x_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}}<\infty
, then by the bounded difference inequality, see for example
[
BLM13
, Theorem 6.2]
, for any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in S_{2}^{m-1}
,
\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\sigma(WX)\right>-{\mathbb{E}}\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\sigma(WX)\right>\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leq\|\sigma\|_{Lip}^{2}\|W\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|x_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}}^{2}
. Furthermore, by the Efron-Stein inequality, see
[
BLM13
, Corollary 3.2]
, combined with
[
FW20
, Equation 34]
, we obtain
\left\|\left<{\boldsymbol{a}},\sigma(WX)\right>\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{Lip}^{2}\|W\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|x_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}}^{2}
. Therefore, when
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{d})
satisfies that all coordinates have
\|x_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}}<\infty
, our results still hold, but with some additional multiplicative factor
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|x_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}}^{2}
, respectively, and in
\bar{p}_{DM}
,
\bar{p}_{DMU}
,
\bar{p}_{RIP}
incurring some logarithmic factors. However, for a general design vector, we may need to examine more carefully whether the
L_{2}
norm is equivalent to the
\psi_{2}
norm. If this equivalence does not hold, then we need to rely on the boundedness of the
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}}
norm rather than the
L_{p}
-
L_{2}
norm equivalence to establish (
33
).

6.5.9
Examples when the spectrum and eigenvectors of the integral operator of the data-dependent conjugate kernel

We provide cases where the spectrum of
\Gamma
can be conveniently estimated. Note that when the spectrum of
\Gamma
is difficult to estimate, we can still use
\Tr(\Gamma)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\Gamma)_{j,j}
as an upper bound, and by choosing an appropriate
\lambda
, we can compute
r_{\lambda,k}^{*}
, following the approach of
[
BBSS22
]
.

1.

When
W=[W(T)|\cdots|W(T)]^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
for some
W(T)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, and
\sigma
satisfies item
2.
of Assumption
6
, by
[
WZ23
, Equation 5.2]
,
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{ij})_{i,j\in[m]}=\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
, where
\|\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}
is the
L_{2}
norm (under standard Gaussian probability measure
\gamma_{d}
) of function
x\in{\mathbb{R}}\mapsto\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}x)
. In particular, when
\|W(T)\|_{2}=1
,
\Gamma_{ij}=\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
for all
i,j\in[m]
. In this case,
\Gamma
is of rank
1
, and the eigenvector associated to the only non-zero eigenvalue
\sigma_{1}(\Gamma)=\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
, is
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}(1,1,\cdots,1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.

2.

When
W=[W_{1}|\cdots|W_{m}]^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
with
\left<W_{i},W_{j}\right>=\delta_{i,j}
for any
i,j\in[m]
. When
\sigma
satisfies item
2.
of Assumption
6
, by
[
WZ23
, Equation 5.2]
,
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{i,j})_{i,j\in[m]}=\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i=j\}}=\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m}I_{m}
. The spectrum of
\Gamma=\{\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m},\cdots,\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m}\}
and the eigenvectors alone are
{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\cdots,{\boldsymbol{e}}_{m}
.

3.

When there exists
J\subset[m]
such that for any
i,j\in J
,
W_{i}=W_{j}={\boldsymbol{w}}
for some
{\boldsymbol{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
; for any
i,j\in J^{c}
,
\left<W_{i},W_{j}\right>=\epsilon_{i}\delta_{ij}
for some
\epsilon_{i}>0
and for any
i\in J^{c},j\in J
,
\left<W_{i},W_{j}\right>=0
. This is a simplified case of the training dynamics studied in
[
BF24
]
. This situation implies that there exists a subset of neurons, that is, neurons
W_{j}
corresponding to
j\in J
, that are well-aligned; the remaining neurons are pairwise orthogonal and orthogonal to the direction of the neurons in
J
. For the case of almost orthogonal neurons, we conjecture that the method of
[
WZ23
, Lemma 5.2]
together with certain matrix perturbation technique could be applied, but this is beyond the scope of this paper. For the sake of simplicity, we assume that
J=[k]
for some
k\in[m]
. Then by
[
WZ23
, Equation 5.2]
,
\Gamma
is a block diagonal matrix with diagonal matrices
\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{k\times k}
, and
\diag(\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma(\epsilon_{i}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}:i\in J^{c})
, where
\mathbbm{1}_{k\times k}
is the
k\times k
matrix with all
1
’s. In this case, the spectrum of
\Gamma
is given by

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{k}{m}\|\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2},\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma(\epsilon_{i}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2},\cdots,\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma(\epsilon_{i}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2},0,0,\cdots,0\bigg\}.

Moreover, the eigenvector associated with eigenvalue
\frac{k}{m}\|\sigma(\|W(T)\|_{2}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
is
(1/\sqrt{k})(\mathbbm{1}_{k}|{\boldsymbol{0}})^{\top}
where
\mathbbm{1}_{k}
is the vector on
1
’s, and other eigenvectors are
({\boldsymbol{0}}|{\boldsymbol{e}}_{i})^{\top}
.

6.5.10
Proof of Proposition
11

Spectrum and eigenfunctions of
\Gamma(T)

In this paragraph, we investigate the spectrum and eigenfunctions of
\Gamma(T)
. We omit
T
from
\Gamma(T)
in the following. Since
X\sim{\cal N}({\boldsymbol{0}},I_{d})
, by
[
WZ23
, Proof of Lemma 5.2]
,
\Gamma
is a diagonal matrix under the basis
\{{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\cdots,{\boldsymbol{e}}_{m}\}
, that is,
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{j,j})_{j\in[m]}=\frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\|W_{j}\|_{2}g)]
, where
g\sim{\cal N}(0,1)
. Since we have that
\delta\in(0,1)\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\delta g)]
is increasing and since we have
\|W_{1}\|_{2}=\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\|_{2}=\cdots=\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{m}\|_{2}=1
by construction, we have:
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(g)]={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\|W_{1}\|_{2}g)]=\sigma_{1}(\Gamma)>\sigma_{2}(\Gamma)=\cdots=\sigma_{m}(\Gamma)=\frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\|W_{2}\|_{2}g)]=\frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\|_{2}g)]
. Therefore, if we take
k=1
, we have:

\displaystyle\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})=\frac{m-1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}g)],\,\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{2})=\frac{m-1}{m^{2}}({\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}g)])^{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}=\frac{1}{m}\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2},\mbox{ and }\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right\|_{\text{op}}=\frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma^{2}(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}g)].

Since
\Gamma
is diagonal along the basis
\{{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\cdots,{\boldsymbol{e}}_{m}\}
, its eigenvectors are given by
\varphi_{1}={\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},\varphi_{2}={\boldsymbol{e}}_{2},\cdots,\varphi_{m}={\boldsymbol{e}}_{m}
.

Remark 7
.

Here we consider a simplified model of the training dynamics of gradient descent. The activation functions we consider are bounded, such as
\tanh
and sigmoid. In the case of an unbounded activation function like ReLU, we can consider
\|W_{1}\|_{2}
to be very large, while
\|W_{2}\|_{2},\cdots,\|W_{m}\|_{2}
are of constant magnitude.

Structure of
W

As we choose
k=1
and
J=\{1\}
, we have
\|W_{J}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1
,
\sum_{j\in J}(\|W_{j}\|_{2}^{2}-1)^{2}\leq(1-\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}})^{2}
and hence we can take
B^{2}=1
.

Checking the conditions

In the following we check Assumption
6
. Item
1.
in Assumption
6
is valid; Item
2.
in Assumption
6
is also valid, since in Assumption
12
, we already assume that
\sigma
is Lipschitz, and from previous analysis, we can take
D=1
. Now we check Item
3.
. We have

1.

\frac{m\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{2})}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\|_{\text{op}}}=(m-1)\frac{\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}\gtrsim 1
if
m\gtrsim\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}})\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{-2}
;

2.

\frac{(\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:m}\right)+\lambda)\left\|\sigma\right\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}N_{2}\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}\|_{\text{op}}}\gtrsim\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}}(\frac{m-1}{N_{2}}+\frac{m\lambda}{N_{2}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}})\gtrsim 1
if either
m\gtrsim N_{2}
, or
\frac{\lambda}{N_{2}}\gtrsim\frac{\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m}
;

3.

\frac{m\sigma_{k}(\Gamma)\sqrt{N_{2}}}{2\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}B^{2}}\gtrsim\frac{m\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma)}^{2}\sqrt{N_{2}}}{\lambda_{\sigma}^{2}m}\gtrsim 1
.

Approximation error

Lemma 17

(Theorem 6.1 of
[
BBSS22
]
)
.

Grant Assumption
12
, we then have: for any
0<\delta<1/4
, with probability at least
\frac{1}{2}-\delta
,

\displaystyle 1-\left|\left<{\boldsymbol{w}}(T),{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*}\right>\right|=\tilde{O}\left(\eta^{-4}\max\left\{\frac{d+m}{N_{1}},\frac{d^{4}}{N_{1}^{2}}\right\}\right).

Let
f^{**}:{\boldsymbol{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mapsto a^{*}\sigma(\left<W(T),{\boldsymbol{x}}\right>)=\left<\phi({\boldsymbol{x}}),{\boldsymbol{a}}^{*}\right>
where
{\boldsymbol{a}}^{*}=\sqrt{m}(a^{*},0,0,\cdots,0)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. By the definition of
f^{**}
and
f^{*}
in Assumption
12
,
(f^{*}-f^{**})({\boldsymbol{x}})=a^{*}(\sigma(\left<W(T),{\boldsymbol{x}}\right>)-\sigma(\left<{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{x}}\right>))
. Because
\sigma
is Lipschitz, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{4}}^{4}

\displaystyle=(a^{*})^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\sigma\left(\left<W(T),G\right>\right)-\sigma\left(\left<{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*},G\right>\right)\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(a^{*})^{4}\left\|\sigma\right\|_{Lip}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left(\left<W(T),G\right>\right)-\left(\left<{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*},G\right>\right)\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=(a^{*})^{4}\left\|\sigma\right\|_{Lip}^{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\left<W(T)-{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*},G\right>\right|^{4}\right]=3(a^{*})^{4}\left\|\sigma\right\|_{Lip}^{4}\left\|W(T)-{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{4}.

Because
\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{w}}^{\perp}}
is the retraction map at
{\boldsymbol{w}}^{\perp}
of the Euclidean sphere (see, for example,
[
Bou23
, Equation 3.40]
), we have
W(T)\in S_{2}^{d-1}
. Hence by Lemma
17
,
\left\|W(T)-{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}=2(1-\left|\left<{\boldsymbol{w}}(T),{\boldsymbol{w}}^{*}\right>\right|)=\tilde{O}\left(\eta^{-4}\max\left\{\frac{d+m}{N_{1}},\frac{d^{4}}{N_{1}^{2}}\right\}\right)
with probability at least
1/2-\delta
. As a result, with probability at least
1/2-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{4}}\lesssim\left|a^{*}\right|\left\|\sigma\right\|_{Lip}\tilde{O}\left(\eta^{-2}\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{d+m}{N_{1}}},\frac{d^{2}}{N_{1}}\right\}\right).

(122)

Estimation error

In the following, we compute
r_{\lambda,k}^{*}
. By our construction of
{\boldsymbol{a}}^{*}
and
\Gamma
, we know that
{\boldsymbol{a}}^{*}\in\Span(\varphi_{1})
and hence
\left\|\Gamma_{1,\mathrm{thre}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1:k}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}(\Gamma)}\left<{\boldsymbol{a}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}\right>^{2}=\frac{m^{2}(a^{*})^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}
, and
\left\|\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{a}}_{k+1:m}^{*}\right\|_{\cal H}^{2}=0
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})}{N_{2}}=\frac{\lambda+\frac{m-1}{m}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{N_{2}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}(\Gamma)=\frac{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m},

if
\lambda\vee\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\lesssim N_{2}/m
. At this time,
\left|J_{1}\right|=1
and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{2})}{\left(\lambda+\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m})\right)^{2}}=\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}(m-1)\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}}{m^{2}\left(\lambda+\frac{m-1}{m}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\right)^{2}}\lesssim\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}}{m\lambda}\wedge\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}}{m-1}.

Hence if

1.

\lambda\vee\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}\lesssim N_{2}/m
,

2.

m\gtrsim\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{-2}
and

3.

\frac{\lambda}{N_{2}}\gtrsim\frac{\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}{m}
or
m\gtrsim N_{2}
,

we have

\displaystyle(r_{\lambda,k}^{*})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{N_{2}}+\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}}{m\lambda}\wedge\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}}{m-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{m^{2}(a^{*})^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{N_{2}^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}(a^{*})^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}\frac{\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}}{N_{2}^{2}}.

We check that the choice for
\lambda=0
,
\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}
such that
\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{-2}\sim m
, and
m\gtrsim N_{2}
satisfy the above constraints and in this case,
(r_{0,1}^{*})^{2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{N_{2}}+\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{N_{2}}{m}+\frac{(a^{*})^{2}}{\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}}\frac{1}{N_{2}^{2}}
.

Probability deviation

1.

By (
119
), the fact that
\|W_{J^{c}}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}=\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
, and
{\mathbb{E}}\|\phi_{k+1:m}(X)\|_{\cal H}^{2}\sim\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
, we know that
\gamma<1/100
provided that
m\gtrsim N_{2}
.

2.

We know that
m^{2}\Tr(\Gamma_{k+1:m}^{2})\sim m\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{4}
and
\|W_{J^{c}}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}=\|\sigma(\delta_{\ref*{delta_feature_learning}}\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(\gamma_{d})}^{2}
, hence
{\gamma_{\ref*{gamma_DMU_L2}}}<1/100
provided that that
m\gtrsim N_{2}
by (
120
).

3.

By (
121
), we know that
\bar{p}_{DM}
,
\bar{p}_{DMU}<1/90
provided that
m\gtrsim N_{2}
.

4.

For
\bar{p}_{RIP}
, since
k=1
is too small in this case, we can set a relatively large
t
by adjusting constant
C_{\ref*{C_vershynin}}
such that
\bar{p}_{RIP}<1/90
, see the discussion after Lemma
16
.

5.

Similarly, for
\bar{p}_{\xi}
, one can modify the constants in Proposition
28
to satisfy
\bar{p}_{\xi}<1/90
. Here, one can use Borel-TIS in (
124
) to get a smaller probability deviation since the noise is assumed to be Gaussian.

6.5.11
Concentration of noise

The following lemma is taken from
[
LS22
, Lemma 10]
.

Lemma 18
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}=(\xi_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
be a random vector with independent mean zero and variance
\sigma_{\xi}
real-valued coordinates. We assume that for all
i
’s,
\left\|\xi_{i}\right\|_{L_{r}}\leq{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}\sigma_{\xi}
for some
\kappa_{2}>0
and
r>4
. There then exists some absolute constant
C_{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}
(depending only on
{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}
) such that for any matrix
D\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times N}
the following holds: if for some integer
k
for which
\sqrt{k}\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\sqrt{\Tr(DD^{\top})}
then with probability at least
1-(C_{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}/k)^{r/4}
,

\left\|D{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{2}\leq(3/2)\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\Tr(DD^{\top})}.

We emphasize that Lemma
18
does not depend on
p
, so we can set
p=\infty.
Note that there exists an isometric embedding from
{\cal H}
to
\ell_{2}
, given by
f\in{\cal H}\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left\langle f,\varphi_{j}\right\rangle_{\cal H}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{j}
, where we recall that
({\boldsymbol{e}}_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}
is ONB of
\ell_{2}
. Therefore, we extend
D:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to\ell_{2}
to
D:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\cal H}
. As a result, we have the following proposition:

Proposition 27
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}=(\xi_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
be a random vector with independent mean zero and variance
\sigma_{\xi}
real-valued coordinates. We assume that for all
i
’s,
\left\|\xi_{i}\right\|_{L_{r}}\leq{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}\sigma_{\xi}
for some
{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}>0
and
r>4
. There then exists some absolute constant
{C_{67}}
(depending only on
{\kappa_{\ref*{kappa_noise}}}
) such that for any Hilbert-Schmidt operator
D:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\to{\cal H}
the following holds: if for some integer
k
for which
\sqrt{k}\left\|D\right\|_{\text{op}}\leq\sqrt{\Tr(DD^{\top})}
then with probability at least
1-(C_{\ref*{C_noise}}/k)^{r/4}
,

\left\|D{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right\|_{\cal H}\leq(3/2)\sigma_{\xi}\sqrt{\Tr(DD^{\top})}.

Proposition 28
.

Let

1.

When
A=({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}\tilde{\Gamma}_{1:k}^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,1:k}^{\top}({\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}{\mathbb{X}}_{\phi,k+1:\infty}^{\top}+\lambda I_{N})^{-1}

&+t_{3}^{2}C_{\ref*{C_noise_dependent_1}}^{2}\frac{\left(\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{2+\varepsilon}}^{2}N\bigg)\\
&\geq 1-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})-\exp\left(-c(t_{1}^{2}\wedge t_{1})\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma^{2}\left(\square,\triangle\right)}\right)-c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}t_{3}^{-(2+\varepsilon)}N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}.\end{aligned}

, (

(b)

When
\varepsilon=0
, (
124
) is still valid with not necessarily
N\geq e^{-1}\left(\exp(2c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}/\varepsilon)-4\right)
, but with
C_{\ref*{C_noise_dependent_3}}
replaced by
32C_{\ref*{C_DMU}}c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}
and with

1
.

Proof.
Recall that
{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}={\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
. We have:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}={\boldsymbol{r}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}+{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}+{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{r}}\leq 2\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{r}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2{\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}.

By Hanson-Wright inequality, see, for example
[
Ver18
, Theorem 6.2.1]
, there exists some absolute constant
c>0
such that for any
t_{1}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\xi}}-\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\Tr(A)\leq t_{1}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\Tr(A)\right)\geq 1-\exp\left(-c(t_{1}^{2}\wedge t_{1})\frac{\Tr(A)}{\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}}\right).

Let
\Omega_{\text{noise},1}
as the random event on which

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Tr(A)}\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}\sqrt{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}},\mbox{ and }\left\|A\right\|_{\text{op}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{4C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)}{4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)}.

By (
52
) and (
53
),
{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{\text{noise},1})\geq 1-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})
.
In Lemma
3
, let
Z=f^{*}(X)-f^{**}(X)
,
q=2+\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon>0
,
r=2
,
p=1
. When
N\geq e^{-1}\left(\exp(2c_{\ref*{c_Men_1}}/\varepsilon)-4\right)
, by Lemma
3
, we have
j_{0}=1
and thus for
\beta=\varepsilon/4
, for any
t_{3}>2
, with probability at least
1-c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}t_{3}^{-(2+\varepsilon)}N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{r}}\right\|_{2}\leq c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}\left(\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2+\varepsilon-2(\varepsilon/4+1)}\right)^{1/2}t_{3}\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{2+\varepsilon}}\sqrt{N}.

Combining the above together, we obtain that for any
t_{1}\geq 0
and
t_{3}>2
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg({\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}^{\top}A{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\leq 2(1+t_{1})\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{16C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}^{2}N\left(\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}\right)}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}+t_{3}^{2}C_{\ref*{C_noise_dependent_1}}^{2}\frac{\left(\sqrt{N}\sigma\left(\square,\triangle\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(4\lambda+\Tr\left(\Gamma_{k+1:\infty}\right)\right)^{2}}\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{2+\varepsilon}}^{2}N\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq 1-{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega_{0}^{c})-\exp\left(-c(t_{1}^{2}\wedge t_{1})\frac{\left|J_{1}\right|\square^{2}+\triangle^{2}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma^{2}\left(\square,\triangle\right)}\right)-c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}t_{3}^{-(2+\varepsilon)}N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}},

where
{C_{69}}=16C_{\ref*{C_RIP_upper}}^{2}c_{\ref*{c_Men_2}}\left(\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2+\varepsilon-2(\varepsilon/4+1)}\right)^{1/2}
. In particular, when we use Markov’s inequality to replace Lemma
3
, with the probability deviation
t_{3}^{-2}
instead of
c_{\ref*{c_Men_3}}t_{3}^{-(2+\varepsilon)}N^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}
, the term
\sqrt{N}\left\|f^{*}-f^{**}\right\|_{L_{2+\varepsilon}}
can be improved to
